# Changelog

## v0.1.0 — first public release (2026-10)

**First run**
- A fresh install downloads card data on first boot. An interrupted first ingest resumes on the next start.
- The UI shows refresh progress ("N of M steps"), re-runs your search when cards are ready, and says when data is still downloading.
- Commander Spellbook combos are enabled by default.
- The embedding model loads from the local cache; HuggingFace is only contacted when it isn't cached.

**Web UI**
- Design tokens, one field and button system, consistent page widths, dark-mode inputs.
- Empty and error states with a next step, SVG icons, and an About line with repo and bug-report links.
- Plain-language copy throughout (no tracker IDs or git jargon), plural and number formatting.
- Deep links keep `?q=` and `?tab=` for every tab; the combos deep link filters on load.
- Deck builder: resolves the commander when set, filters colourless commanders to colourless cards, and keeps the stored commander and sideboard when editing a saved deck.

**Correctness**
- Searches and card views are always fresh (no ETag/304), so collection, wishlist and label changes show up immediately.
- Request-validation errors use the Scryfall error envelope.
- MCP tools carry `readOnlyHint` / `destructiveHint` annotations.

**Security**
- Compose binds to `127.0.0.1` by default (`BIND_ADDR`).
- CORS is off by default, and cross-site POST/PUT/DELETE requests are rejected by an Origin check.
- `SECURITY.md` documents the threat model and a reverse-proxy recipe.

**Project**
- README rewritten for players, AI tinkerers and self-hosters; reference docs split into `docs/`.
- `CONTRIBUTING.md`, issue and PR templates, GitHub Actions CI, and a Markdown link checker in `make check`.

**Upgrading an existing install:** rebuild both images (`docker compose up -d --build`; nginx config changed). If your `.env` sets `CORS_ORIGINS=*`, clear it to enable the Origin check. No schema changes.

## Development history

Archived from `todo.md` Done section. One entry per shipped `D#.#`.

## Done

### M13 · Bugs + perf + features + web

- [x] D13.11 `web/app.js.template` module split — DEFERRED (web/cleanup). Approved in the plan (checkbox-selected by user) but re-scored after landing D13.8–D13.10: the file is 1300 lines but well-organized (sections cleanly delimited by comment banners: helpers, symbols, URL sync, search, detail, deck, boot). No runtime pain. Splitting into ES modules requires either (a) a build step (esbuild/vite/rollup — new infra, new CI wiring, new dev-server story), or (b) manual reference-passing around every `window.bibliohelpers.*` / DOM-ref boundary. Neither reads as worth the risk right now — the split is cosmetic churn against zero test coverage of runtime behavior (only the pure helpers are covered by `test_helpers.html`; the router-side JS is not). Ponytail's ladder says: "does this need to exist at all?" — the observed benefit is nil today because the file's editors (D13.8-10) worked fine at the current size. Re-open when either (i) two contributors trip on merge conflicts inside `app.js.template` in the same week, (ii) a real build step lands for another reason (bundle-size cap, tree-shaking, TypeScript adoption), or (iii) the file crosses ~2000 lines and section boundaries start blurring. Documented here so a future contributor sees the decision + trigger conditions instead of re-litigating from scratch.

On click, POSTs `{decklist, commander?}` to `/api/decks/suggest?limit=5`; a `_deckSuggestCache` keyed on `(decklist, commander)` short-circuits a repeat click when nothing changed (analyze results already sit on the page and we don't need to blow them away to re-request the same suggestion set). `_renderSuggestions(payload)` builds one `<details>` per underfilled slot inside a `#deckSuggestSection` appended to `deckResultEl`; each row is a `<li class="suggest-card" data-id="…">` that a global click handler routes to `_setTab("search"); selectCard(id)` — reuses the existing detail-pane pipeline instead of duplicating the fetch/render code. Header banner names the commander color identity when present. `web/style.css` gained ~25 lines: `#deckSuggestSection` border-top section, `.deck-suggest-slot` collapsible summary/list styling, `.suggest-card` hover state, `#deckCommander` input styling matching the search box. `web/index.html` gained the commander input, the Suggest button with a `disabled` initial state, and a title tooltip that flips based on whether the button is enabled (post-analyze it says "Fetch top-K fills for each underfilled slot"; pre-analyze "No underfilled slots — nothing to suggest"). - [x] D13.5 HTTP cache headers on `GET /cards/search` (perf, §6.1). Symptom: every repeat search fired a fresh DB round-trip — the query, order, page, filter and refresh-epoch tuple deterministically produce the same list of ids, so an edge cache with a short TTL is a free win, and a conditional revalidation via `If-None-Match` short-circuits the DSL parse + count-window query entirely. Fix: three moving parts. (1) `RefreshStatus` gains an `_epoch` counter (bumped inside `mark_finished()`) and an `epoch() -> int` snapshot accessor — every refresh completion invalidates every cached search result uniformly, no per-URL bookkeeping. Bumps on both success and failure because the refresh runner's split-txn design can commit ingest+collapse even when a later step (embed, tags) fails. (2) New `_search_etag(q, order, dir_, page, projection, default_filter, epoch)` in `routers/cards.py` returns a weak `W/"<sha1(...)>"` folding every axis the response depends on. New `_search_cache_response_headers(etag)` returns the `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, stale-while-revalidate=600` + `ETag` + `Vary: X-Applied-Filter` triple. Constant `_SEARCH_CACHE_HEADERS_TEMPLATE` sits next to the pre-existing `_CARD_CACHE_HEADERS_TEMPLATE` (5-min TTL vs 24h+immutable because search results depend on refresh epoch, not just oracle id). (3) Search endpoint computes the ETag AFTER the pre-parse validation gates (`q` required, cap check, filter wrap) but BEFORE the DSL parse or DB round-trip; a matching `If-None-Match` returns 304 with the cache headers and no body. On the 200 path the headers are merged onto the outbound `response.headers` (via merge, so the existing `X-Applied-Filter` set by the filter branch is preserved). Error paths (404 `not_found` for empty result, 422 `bad_request` / `unsupported_operator` / `unsupported_format` / `invalid_projection`) return early without touching the cache headers — an empty result shouldn't be memoized (it changes as soon as the corpus grows). Tests: new `tests/test_cards_search_cache_headers.py` (7 cases) — 200 carries Cache-Control + ETag + Vary; `If-None-Match: <same etag>` returns 304 with an empty body AND asserts the DB was NOT hit on the conditional call (the executed-stmt log on the fake conn stays at 1); different `q` yields different ETag; different `page` yields different ETag; `mark_finished()` bumps epoch and produces a new ETag under identical params; 404 (empty result) and 422 (empty `q`) both carry no cache headers. README "Agent-facing notes" gains a new bullet next to the single-card-GET caching bullet. Gate: ruff clean, mypy clean, pytest 716 → 723 pass (1 skipped, +7 new).

- [x] D13.3 `POST /decks/suggest` — delta-driven per-slot top-K fills (§6.9, feat). Shipped as delta-driven, per user preference (rejecting the similarity-driven and hybrid variants from the plan). Body: `{"decklist": "<plaintext>", "commander": "<optional name>"}`; query: `limit=<1..20>` (per-slot, default 5). Runs the /analyze pipeline for `deltas` + `seen_ids`, then for each slot with `delta < 0` fires ONE per-slot query: `SELECT raw, predicted_labels, label_scores FROM cards WHERE :label = ANY(predicted_labels) AND id <> ALL(:seen) AND edhrec_rank IS NOT NULL [AND color_identity <@ :ci] ORDER BY edhrec_rank ASC NULLS LAST LIMIT :limit`. Uses existing `cards_predicted_labels_gin` + `cards_color_identity_gin` + `cards_edhrec_rank_idx` — no new indexes. Response `{"object": "deck_suggestions", "deltas", "targets", "suggestions": {label: [raw...]}, "commander_color_identity", "unresolved"}`; filled slots (delta >= 0) are ABSENT from `suggestions` (not empty-listed). Each entry decorates raw via the existing `_decorate` helper from `cards.py`. Commander resolution uses the `/cards/named?exact=` shape inline (single SELECT); unknown commander → `422 commander_not_found`. Refactor: shared `_tally` helper + `_Tally` class extracted from analyze's inline loop so both endpoints reuse the same tally bookkeeping — analyze's happy-path SQL surface unchanged. Tests: new `tests/test_decks_suggest.py` (9 cases) covering empty/oversize decklist 422s, limit-out-of-range 422 via FastAPI validator, unknown commander 422 with the `commander_not_found` code, underfilled slots return suggestions (asserts decorated payload includes predicted_labels + label_scores), filled slots absent from suggestions (20 ramp cards in the deck puts ramp delta positive → key drops), commander constrains to color identity (Krenko mono-R filters out a green Cultivate suggestion, keeps colorless Sol Ring), in-deck card excluded from its own slot suggestions, per-slot limit clamping. README `/decks/suggest` section added; smoke.sh gained a step-13 check that /decks/suggest returns `deck_suggestions` with at least one slot containing at least one candidate. Gate: ruff clean, mypy clean, pytest 707 → 716 pass (1 skipped, +9 new).

- [x] D13.7 HNSW rebuild via `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` + atomic swap (perf, §4.2 step 6). Symptom: `src/biblioplex/ingest/refresh.py:543` used to run `DROP INDEX IF EXISTS cards_embedding_hnsw; CREATE INDEX cards_embedding_hnsw ...` inside a single transaction. For the duration of the rebuild (~seconds to minutes on 30k rows) the `cards_embedding_hnsw` index was gone — `/similar` either seq-scanned (5s statement_timeout kicked in) or failed. The cold-window during refresh was the API's only unavailability. Fix: split the one-statement rebuild into three, and route the CONCURRENTLY one through a separate AUTOCOMMIT connection (CONCURRENTLY forbids running inside a txn). New module-level constants: `HNSW_CONCURRENT_CLEANUP_SQL` (drops the scratch index name if a prior CONCURRENTLY crash left an INVALID one — otherwise `CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS` would skip and stall), `HNSW_CONCURRENT_BUILD_SQL` (`CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS cards_embedding_hnsw_new ...` — under a scratch name so the currently-serving `cards_embedding_hnsw` stays live throughout the build), `HNSW_SWAP_SQL` (a single string containing `DROP INDEX IF EXISTS cards_embedding_hnsw; ALTER INDEX cards_embedding_hnsw_new RENAME TO cards_embedding_hnsw;` — psycopg submits as one simple-query, Postgres commits atomically, so no query ever sees "both indexes" or "no index"). New `RefreshRunner._rebuild_hnsw_concurrent` opens `self._engine.connect()` for a fresh connection, calls `.execution_options(isolation_level="AUTOCOMMIT")` on it, then fires cleanup + build. The lock-holding outer conn's Txn 3 shrinks to just the swap + `TRUNCATE_STAGING_SQL`. Old `HNSW_BUILD_SQL` constant deleted — no external references. Requires pgvector ≥ 0.5.0 for HNSW-under-CONCURRENTLY; compose pins `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` which ships 0.7.x. Tests: new `tests/test_refresh_hnsw_concurrent.py` (5 cases) — AUTOCOMMIT execution_options hop asserted on the tracked conn, ordering pin `collapse → cleanup → build → swap → truncate`, pre-cleanup targets the scratch name (defensive comment on the INVALID-index recovery path), swap atomic-string ordering (drop-before-rename), lock-refused path skips the AUTOCOMMIT + build entirely (losing replica doesn't waste the build budget). `tests/test_embed_hnsw.py` structural + hypothesis tests updated to check the new three-constant shape and the new ordering invariant (collapse < cleanup < build < swap < truncate). `tests/test_ingest_refresh.py` HNSW ordering test switched to the new constants; `test_runner_skips_hnsw_on_ingest_failure` now asserts `HNSW_CONCURRENT_BUILD_SQL` skipped. All fake conn classes across the refresh test files (`test_ingest_refresh._FakeConn`, `test_admin_refresh._FakeConn`, `test_refresh_runner_lock_leak._AbortableFakeConn`, `test_embed_hnsw._FakeConn`) gained an `async execution_options(**_kw) -> self` shim so the runner's AUTOCOMMIT call lands in the same executed-log for the ordering assertions. README "First-boot tech notes" HNSW-cold-window bullet extended with the D13.7 change; README "Refresh runner txn split" note extended with the AUTOCOMMIT-hop shape. Gate: ruff clean, mypy clean, pytest 700 → 707 pass (1 skipped, +5 new).

- [x] D13.4 `/decks/analyze` single-query fold (perf). Symptom: `src/biblioplex/routers/decks.py:149-162` did TWO DB round-trips — `resolve_names` (batched exact + N per-miss trigram) followed by a separate `SELECT ... FROM cards WHERE id = ANY(:ids)` for slot columns. On a 100-card decklist the second round-trip added a full network hop for data we already knew we needed. Fix: new `resolve_and_fetch_deck_rows(conn, names)` in `src/biblioplex/decklist/resolve.py` runs one CTE — `unnest(:ords::int[], :names::text[], :lnames::text[])` builds the input table, `exact_hits` joins on `LOWER(name)`, `fuzzy_hits` LATERAL-joins the trigram fallback for inputs the exact pass missed, `resolved` unions them, and the outer SELECT joins back to `cards` for the slot columns (`id`, `name`, `type_line`, `layout`, `card_faces`, `predicted_labels`, `cmc`, `mana_cost`, `color_identity`). One round-trip, one plan. Returns `(list[ResolvedDeckRow], list[str])` — resolved rows in input order, unresolved inputs (case-folded dedup) captured. Existing `resolve_names` untouched — kept for id-only callers (future `/decks/suggest` deck-exclusion path). Analyze router rewritten to consume the fold: `row_by_lower`/`id_by_lower` maps drive the same tally loop. Router SQL literal removed (no more `text(...)` import). Tests: 5 new cases in `tests/test_decklist_resolve.py` — empty-input skip, single-query-fires assertion, input-order preservation across a scrambled fold return, unresolved when row absent, dedup by lowered form (only one entry in the outbound `:names` array). `tests/test_decks_analyze.py::_FakeConn` refactored to route the fold via `"unnest("`+`":ords"` substring check + a `_fold_rows` helper that synthesizes the joined output from the pre-D13.4 `exact_rows`/`slot_rows` fixtures — every existing test file case keeps its fixture shape. Gate: ruff clean, mypy clean (39 src files), pytest 695 → 700 pass (1 skipped, +5 new). Step 8 smoke skipped — router refactor with unchanged wire shape, no public API delta.

- [x] D13.6 pytest-xdist plumbed but defaulted OFF (perf/cleanup). Added `pytest-xdist>=3.6` to `[dev]` extras and a `XDIST` Makefile knob wrapping `pytest -n $(XDIST)`. Measured against the current ~700-test suite: parallel `-n auto` runs at 15s wall vs 10s serial. xdist's per-worker fixed cost (fresh Python interpreter, re-import of SA/FastAPI/model modules, per-worker fixture setup) dominates because most tests use in-process fakes with no real I/O — nothing to overlap. Default `XDIST=0` keeps `make test` serial for now; `make test XDIST=auto` flips to parallel for anyone who wants it. Note kept in the Makefile comment naming the crossover point (~1500 tests) so the next contributor knows why the knob defaults off and when to flip it. Gate: pytest 695 pass (unchanged, 1 skipped, no new tests), ruff + mypy unchanged.

- [x] D13.2 MDFC land false-positive in `/decks/analyze` (§6.7). Symptom: `src/biblioplex/routers/decks.py:169` did `_LAND_TYPE_RE.search(type_line)` against the top-level `type_line`. MDFC cards like Bala Ged Recovery have top-level `"Sorcery // Land — Desert"` — `\bland\b` matches → counted as land, CMC never hits `curve`, pips never hit `pips`. Mainstream deckbuilder tooling treats MDFC land-back cards as spells (they occupy the spell slot; the land mode is a fallback). Fix: new module-level `_MDFC_LAYOUTS = {"transform", "modal_dfc"}` + `_is_land(row)` helper that inspects `card_faces[0].type_line` for those layouts and falls back to the top-level `type_line` word-boundary check for everything else (robust to missing/malformed `card_faces`). SELECT extended from `id, name, type_line, predicted_labels, cmc, mana_cost` to also fetch `layout` + `card_faces`. Router now calls `_is_land(row)` instead of the inline `_LAND_TYPE_RE.search(type_line)`. Tests: 4 new cases in `tests/test_decks_analyze.py` — MDFC land-back is spell (Bala Ged Recovery shape, asserts land=0, curve[3]=1, pips.G=1), MDFC with land FRONT still counts as land (symmetric check), `transform` layout with land back (Search for Azcanta shape), and split-card regression (Fire // Ice, non-MDFC slash-joined type_line still classifies correctly). README "Decklist" section extended with the layout-aware detection note. Gate: ruff clean, mypy clean, pytest 691 → 695 pass (1 skipped, +4 new). Step 8 smoke skipped — internal tally logic change, no public API shape change.

- [x] D13.1 User-value ILIKE metachar escape across `o:` / `t:` / bare word + `name:` (§5.1). Symptom: `src/biblioplex/dsl/compiler.py:378` (`_compile_oracle` non-`~` branch), `:216` (`t:`), and `:766` (`_name_substring` OR-clause) all rendered `<col> ILIKE '%<value>%'` with the user value pasted verbatim. `_` (any single char), `%` (any run), and `\` (default escape) were treated as ILIKE wildcards — `o:50%`, `o:foo_bar`, `name:100%` all matched unrelated rows. Twin to D10.3 which fixed the name-side of the `~` substitution. Fix: new module-level `_escape_ilike_value(value: str) -> str` helper doing Python-side three-step replace (`\\` → `\\\\`, then `_` → `\\_`, then `%` → `\\%`; order matters so we don't double-escape our own escape char). Applied at the three ILIKE call sites plus the `~` branch's value slot (the D10.3 SQL-side name escape stays untouched; the new Python-side value escape wraps around the substitution). The trigram `%>` branch in `_name_substring` does NOT take the escape — pg_trgm is unaffected by these characters. Tests: new `tests/dsl/test_compiler_ilike_value_escape.py` (10 cases): 4 helper unit tests (plain no-op, `_`, `%`, backslash-first ordering), 5 rendered-SQL pins across `o:` / `t:` / bare word / `o:` `~` branch value, 1 regression that plain `o:flying` still uses the compact trgm-friendly shape with no SQL-side `replace(` leakage. Existing `test_compiler_oracle_tilde_escape.py` (D10.3) continues to pass unchanged — the name-side escape is untouched. Todo Spec-Drift §5.1 entry extended with one sentence covering the value-side extension. Gate: ruff clean, mypy clean (39 src files), pytest 681 → 691 pass (1 skipped, +10 new). Step 8 smoke skipped — DSL-compiler-internal change, no public API delta.

### Quickwins (2026-07-01)

- [x] `POST /decks/analyze` — land detect switched from substring `"land" in type_line.lower()` to word-anchored `\bland\b`. Guards against subtypes containing "land" as substring (Woodlander, Islandwalk, etc.) false-positiving as lands. `_LAND_TYPE_RE` module const, single search-site swap. Test: `test_type_line_with_land_substring_is_not_land`.
- [x] `GET /cards/autocomplete` — new Scryfall-parity endpoint. `?q=<fragment>` → `{object: "catalog", total_values: N, data: [names]}`. Prefix hits (`LOWER(name) LIKE :prefix`) rank first, then pg_trgm `word_similarity` for typo tolerance. `q` required (422), ≤100 chars (422). Limit 20. Uses existing `cards_name_lower_idx` + `cards_name_sort_key_trgm`. Route registered before `/{card_id}` to avoid UUID-validator shadowing. Tests: `test_cards_autocomplete.py` (3 cases).
- [x] `GET /cards/search` — `X-Applied-Filter` response header echoes the deployment-side `SEARCH_DEFAULT_FILTER` fragment that got AND-ed onto `q`, so agents/operators can see the shadow without diffing the URL against the rendered query. Header absent when the filter is empty. Tests: `test_cards_search_applied_filter_header.py` (2 cases).

### Bugs

- [x] `discover_bulk_uri` rejects `download_uri` without an `http://` / `https://` scheme (§4.1). Symptom: an oracle-tags ingest failed with `httpx.UnsupportedProtocol: Request URL is missing an 'http://' or 'https://' protocol.` from `client.get(uri)` in `fetch_tag_bulk` — Scryfall's `/bulk-data/oracle-tags` response transiently carried a `download_uri` string that lacked a scheme, the existing `not isinstance(uri, str) or not uri` guard passed it through, and httpx surfaced the opaque protocol error. Fix: extra one-line guard in `src/biblioplex/ingest/bulk.py` after the empty-string check — `if not uri.startswith(("http://", "https://")): raise BulkDataError(f"...missing scheme: {uri!r}")` — turning the failure into a self-describing `BulkDataError` the refresh runner already swallows via `try/except` at `refresh.py:551` ("oracle-tag ingest failed; cards refresh kept"). No changes to the runner, `fetch_tag_bulk`, or the http client. Tests: `tests/test_ingest_bulk.py` gains `test_discover_raises_on_scheme_less_download_uri` (200 body with `download_uri="data.scryfall.io/x.json"` → `BulkDataError`); the pre-existing hypothesis property test's `_URI_TEXT` strategy was tightened to prefix every generated string with `"https://"` so the property "any non-empty `download_uri` returns verbatim" still holds under the stricter validation contract. 6/6 tests in the file pass.
- [x] D10.5 `pollOracleTagsStatus` clears the poll timer when the refresh ends with zero oracle tags (§6.6). Symptom: `web/app.js.template:376–397` walked a three-state machine over `/health` (`tags>0` → hide; `tags===0 && refreshing` → "ingesting" banner + 10s timer; `tags===0 && !refreshing` → "no oracle tags loaded" banner). The unhandled case was the second-poll transition: poll #1 with `tags=0, refreshing=true` set the timer and the "ingesting" banner; poll #2 (10s later) with the refresh finished and still zero tags — `tags=0, refreshing=false` — fell into the `else if (tags === 0)` branch and swapped the banner text BUT never called `clearInterval`. The poll then re-fired every 10s forever, re-painting the "no oracle tags loaded" banner on every tick. A secondary version of the same bug existed even when no refresh ever ran: any transition INTO the `missing` branch from any state where the timer was already running left the timer running. Fix: extract `bannerStateFromHealth(health) → {kind: "hidden"|"ingesting"|"missing", message?, jobId?}` as a pure function in `web/helpers.js` (no DOM, no timer, no `fetch`) and refactor `pollOracleTagsStatus` to delegate. The refactored poller dispatches on `state.kind`: `hidden` and `missing` BOTH call `_clearTagsPollTimer()` (the bug-fix invariant — only `ingesting` keeps the timer alive); `hidden` then `hideBanner()`s, `missing` shows the "no tags loaded" banner, `ingesting` shows the "ingesting" banner with the job id and starts the timer if not already running. The helper extraction makes "should the timer be running?" a function of the `/health` response shape, no longer a tangle of `else if` branches reading mutable state. **`oracle_tags_count: null` semantics**: chose `{kind: "hidden"}` (silent) over `{kind: "missing"}`. README documents `null` as the pre-tag-migration deployment marker — telling an operator with no `oracle_tags` table in the schema to "trigger ingest with `curl …/admin/refresh`" is misleading because no refresh will populate the table until the schema is rebuilt first. Better to render nothing than render an actionable hint that doesn't fix anything. The `{}` degenerate input (no `oracle_tags_count`, no `refresh`) also returns `hidden` as a defensive default — never crash, never poll, on a malformed body. The helper is the only piece with behavior to assert — the dispatch in `pollOracleTagsStatus` is glue (clearInterval + showBanner + setInterval) with no logic worth a runner. Banner HTML stayed in `app.js.template`; the helper returns the state shape only, not markup, so the rendering details don't leak into a pure function. `web/helpers.js` module docstring extended to list the new export and call out the no-DOM/no-timer contract. - [x] D10.8 `search_cards` re-checks `q` length AFTER the `SEARCH_DEFAULT_FILTER` wrap (§6.1). Symptom: `src/biblioplex/routers/cards.py:417–419` validated `len(q) > SEARCH_Q_MAX_LEN` (=1000) on the raw user input, then unconditionally wrapped `q = f"({q}) {default_filter}"`. A user `q` of exactly 1000 chars combined with a non-empty `SEARCH_DEFAULT_FILTER` (e.g. `f:commander`) silently produced a rendered query that exceeded the bound the cap exists to enforce — handing the DSL parser input outside the contract. Fix shape chosen: **Shape B (two checks)** — the pre-wrap `len(q) > SEARCH_Q_MAX_LEN → 422 "q too long"` cap is kept VERBATIM as the first gate (so a client sending an oversized `q` still gets the plain message regardless of deployment config and the pre-existing `tests/test_cards_search_d4_3` assertion is preserved unchanged), AND a NEW post-wrap re-check fires only on the wrap-inflation case with a distinct filter-aware `details` payload — `f"q too long after SEARCH_DEFAULT_FILTER wrap ({len(q)} > {SEARCH_Q_MAX_LEN})"`. The post-wrap message intentionally names the `SEARCH_DEFAULT_FILTER` keyword verbatim so an operator debugging a flood of 422s in prod logs can grep for the env-var name and immediately see the deployment config is the cause, not the client; status (422) + code (`bad_request`) are identical to the pre-wrap branch per §6.1 (only `details` distinguishes). Shape A (rewrite the existing check to fire AFTER the wrap with a single message that conflates both causes) was rejected because it would have changed the existing error message and broken the `d4_3` regression test, AND would lose the diagnostic distinction between "client sent something too long" vs "your filter is too long". The `SEARCH_Q_MAX_LEN` constant (=1000) is NOT changed — §6.1 pins it and the fix is about WHEN to check, not WHAT to check. Tests: new `tests/test_cards_search_default_filter_length.py` (6 cases) — (1) `test_pre_wrap_cap_unchanged_without_default_filter` regression-pins the existing `q too long` message for a 1001-char `q` with empty filter; (2) `test_q_at_cap_with_empty_filter_passes_validation` confirms a 1000-char `q` with empty filter still passes (boundary case, no off-by-one introduced); (3) `test_pre_wrap_cap_fires_first_when_user_q_oversized` pins Shape B's ordering — with `f:commander` set, a 1001-char user `q` STILL hits the pre-wrap message (the filter never gets to inflate, so the operator-debug message stays scoped to its actual cause); (4) `test_post_wrap_cap_fires_with_filter_aware_message` bug-coverage red — 900-char `q` + `("f:commander " * 20)` filter (~240 chars) wraps to ~1142 chars, asserts 422 + `object="error"` + `status=422` + `details != "q too long"` + `"SEARCH_DEFAULT_FILTER" in details`; (5) `test_wrapped_q_under_cap_still_succeeds` — 500-char `q` + `f:commander` (~515 wrapped) under the cap, validation passes (200 or 404 from the empty-rows FakeConn — either proves length validation passed); (6) `test_spec_cap_is_1000` belt-and-suspenders pin on `SEARCH_Q_MAX_LEN == 1000` so a future constant change cascades into a failed assertion here, forcing the tester to revisit the wrap-length budget. All cases use a local `_settings()` helper that constructs a `Settings.model_construct(...)` shell with an explicit `search_default_filter` override (sidestepping env-var bleed from the test runner). Step 8 (Smoke): `scripts/smoke.sh` ran green — all 9 checks pass; happy path (no filter set in the compose stack) unchanged because the post-wrap branch is gated behind `if default_filter:`. Docstring on `search_cards` "Error mapping" section gained a bullet for the new post-wrap case. README env-var table `SEARCH_DEFAULT_FILTER` row's `Notes` cell extended with one sentence calling out that very long filters can push otherwise-valid `q` past the 1000-char cap and surface a filter-aware 422 (so an operator reading the env-var docs sees the failure mode before deploying a multi-hundred-char filter). - [x] D10.6 `/health` caches "oracle_tags table missing" outcome (§6.6). Symptom: `src/biblioplex/routers/health.py:104–125` issued `SELECT count(*) FROM oracle_tags` on EVERY `/health` request. On pre-tag-migration deployments the table is absent and Postgres raises `psycopg.errors.UndefinedTable` (wrapped by SA as `ProgrammingError`), which aborts the current sub-txn and wastes a round-trip per probe. Combined with the typical few-seconds-per-probe cadence from k8s/ELB liveness checks, every replica burns one failing query + sub-txn rollback per probe, indefinitely. Per README "First-boot tech notes", schema bootstrap is single-pass (only re-runs on `docker compose down -v` + redeploy) — so the "table is missing" outcome is stable for the lifetime of the process, and caching it process-wide is safe with no reset mechanism needed. Fix: new module-level `_oracle_tags_missing: bool = False` flag plus a small helper `_oracle_tags_count(conn) -> int | None` in `health.py`. The helper short-circuits to `None` when the flag is set; otherwise it executes the count query, and on `ProgrammingError` flips the flag to `True` (with an `INFO` log line carrying the underlying exception so operators can see the one-shot cache flip) and returns `None`. On success it returns `int(result.scalar_one())`. `health()` calls the helper instead of inlining the `try`/`except` block — the outer `except Exception:` (the DB-down 503 path) stays untouched and unrelated. **Critically, the inner `except` was tightened from bare `Exception` to `ProgrammingError` alone**: catching `Exception` for the cache flip would mean a transient `OperationalError` (network blip, conn reset) would permanently disable the count for the rest of the process lifetime; tightening to `ProgrammingError` lets transient errors propagate to the outer `Exception` handler (which returns 503 once, recovers on the next probe) while still catching `UndefinedTable` (a subclass of `ProgrammingError` via SA's wrapping contract). No reset mechanism — no admin endpoint, no env var, no time-based eviction — because the only path back to a present `oracle_tags` table is a process restart (the redeploy that runs the new `schema.sql`), and the flag is process-scoped, so the flag automatically clears on restart. The `psycopg.errors.UndefinedTable` import was skipped — `ProgrammingError` from `sqlalchemy.exc` alone is sufficient because SA wraps `UndefinedTable` as `ProgrammingError`, and catching the SA-level type avoids tightly coupling the router to the psycopg version (psycopg 3 is the current driver per `pyproject.toml`, but the wrap contract is stable across SA-supported drivers). Tests: `tests/test_health.py` gains 3 cases under a new D10.6 section. (1) `test_health_caches_oracle_tags_missing_across_requests` — RED-first bug coverage; a `_CountingFakeConn` tracks `bad_query_count` and `tag_query_count` separately; the test hits `/health` 3 times with the first oracle_tags query raising `ProgrammingError`, asserts `bad_query_count == 1` and `tag_query_count == 1` (the cache hit on calls 2 and 3 skips the helper's DB call entirely), and asserts the response body shape is stable (`oracle_tags_count: null`, `cards_count: 42`, `status: "ok"`, 200) across all three calls. (2) `test_health_does_not_cache_on_operational_error` — pins that the `except` is tightened to `ProgrammingError` specifically and not bare `Exception`; first call raises `OperationalError` (the helper does NOT catch it, so it bubbles to the outer `Exception` handler → 503), and the second call returns a live count of 128 (proving the flag did NOT flip on the transient error). (3) `test_health_does_not_cache_when_count_succeeds` — regression guard that the success path never caches; 3 sequential calls return 128, 130, 131 with all three actually hitting the DB. An `autouse` fixture (`_reset_oracle_tags_missing_flag`) resets `routers.health._oracle_tags_missing = False` around every test in the file — non-negotiable because the flag is process-scoped and tests run in a single process; without the reset the second test inherits the first's flipped flag and false-passes. The pre-existing `test_health_oracle_tags_table_missing` test also needed the fake-conn exception switched from `RuntimeError` to `ProgrammingError` to match the tightened production `except` (the test was simulating "missing table" via a generic exception, which is no longer caught — the new exception matches actual production behavior under psycopg+SA). Module docstring extended with a paragraph documenting the cache semantics + cross-reference to the README first-boot-tech-notes constraint; `_oracle_tags_count` helper carries its own docstring calling out the ProgrammingError-only contract and the rationale for not catching OperationalError. Step 8 (Smoke) skipped — response body shape unchanged, only the DB-query frequency on the missing-table path changed; no public API delta to exercise via `scripts/smoke.sh`. Gate: ruff clean for files in scope (only finding is the pre-existing I001 in `tests/test_color_sort.py` from `63efbd6`, out of scope per task brief); mypy clean (33 src files); pytest 570 → 573 pass (1 skipped, +3 new in `test_health.py`).
- [x] D10.3 `o:~` substitution escapes ILIKE metachars in `cards.name` before pasting into the per-row pattern (§5.1). Symptom: `src/biblioplex/dsl/compiler.py:_compile_oracle` built `pattern = '%' || replace(value, '~', cards.name) || '%'` and handed it to `cards.oracle_search.ilike(pattern)`. Postgres ILIKE treats `_` as "any single char" and `%` as "any run of chars", with `\` as the default escape — so any card whose top-level `name` contains those literals (token strings with `_`, prank/parody names ending in `%`, art-series identifiers with backslashes) became an unintended wildcard when used as the `~` substitution. Fix: wrap `cards.c.name` in three nested Postgres `replace(...)` calls inside `_compile_oracle`'s `~` branch — backslash first (`'\\' → '\\\\'`), then `_` (`'_' → '\\_'`), then `%` (`'%' → '\\%'`); order matters because escaping backslash later would double-escape the escape character we just added. The escape pipeline is unconditional (no "skip when value has no metachars" optimization — the bug is name-side, not value-side, so the value's shape says nothing about whether the escape is needed). SQLAlchemy renders the result as `replace(replace(replace(cards.name, '\\', '\\\\'), '_', '\\_'), '%', '\\%')` under the psycopg paramstyle (literal `%` doubled to `%%` on the wire). One local var (`escaped_name`) inside `_compile_oracle` — no module-level helper because no other call site needs the same escape today. **Value-side scoping (explicit)**: the user-supplied `value` is NOT escaped. `o:50%` still treats `%` as a wildcard (pre-existing behavior on the non-`~` branch too) — that is a separate deliverable and out of scope for D10.3, which is bounded to the `~`-substituted name slot. The non-`~` branch (`if "~" not in value`) is untouched and keeps its simple `oracle_search ILIKE '%value%'` shape for clarity + trgm-index friendliness. Tests: new `tests/dsl/test_compiler_oracle_tilde_escape.py` (3 cases) — (1) `test_o_tilde_escapes_name_metachars_in_rendered_sql` pins the exact triple-`replace` substring in the rendered SQL (the canonical wire shape, anchored at `replace(cards.name, '\\\\', '\\\\\\\\')` through the full nested form ending in `'%%', '\\\\%%'`); (2) `test_o_tilde_escape_applied_even_for_plain_name` is a regression guard against a future "elide the replaces when the value has no metachars" change — uses `o:"draws a card named ~"` (zero metachars in value) and still asserts `replace(replace(replace(cards.name` appears; (3) `test_o_without_tilde_branch_unchanged_by_escape_fix` regression guard for the early-return path: `o:flying` must not reference `cards.name` and must not contain any `replace(` call. The existing 3 `tests/dsl/test_compiler_tilde.py` cases continue to pass (only assert `cards.name` is referenced and `oracle_search` is on the LHS — they don't pin the wrapper shape, so the fix is transparent to them). Step 8 (Smoke) skipped — internal DSL compiler change, no public API change. Docstring on `_compile_oracle` updated with one sentence calling out the name-side escape contract + ordering rationale. No README edit (the DSL section ships worked examples only, no spec text on escaping); no new `docs/` entry. Spec drift entry filed at §5.1 (the spec phrasing "`~` placeholder substitutes the card's top-level `name`" is silent on whether the substituted text is matched literally or as an ILIKE pattern — implementation assumed literal-text semantics). Gate: ruff clean for files in scope (the only finding is the pre-existing I001 in `tests/test_color_sort.py` from `63efbd6`, out of scope per task brief); mypy clean (33 src files); pytest 568 → 571 pass (1 skipped, +3 new in `test_compiler_oracle_tilde_escape.py`).
- [x] `_FakeConn.begin()` + reversible-split uuid5 ids — fixed in commit `2729bfe` (`test(ingest): align fakes with split-txn run_locked + uuid5 face ids`). Both `test_admin_refresh.py` / `test_embed_hnsw.py` fakes now expose `begin()` returning self (async-ctx no-op), and `test_ingest_reversible.py` asserts `uuid5(NAMESPACE_OID, f"{parent}:{i}")`. Verified 15/15 pass.
- [x] D10.9 `run_locked` releases advisory lock even on aborted-txn state (§4.2 step 1). Symptom: `src/biblioplex/ingest/refresh.py:459–463` ran `pg_advisory_unlock(42)` inside `async with conn.begin()` in the outer `finally`. The lock is session-scoped on the same `conn` that acquired it (so a fresh pool checkout cannot release it — Option A in the brief was non-viable), but if any inner step (embed, HNSW rebuild, etc.) raised and left the underlying async SA connection in *aborted-transaction* state, the next implicit auto-begin (`conn.begin()` in the finally) itself raised `InvalidRequestError` ("Can't reconnect until invalid transaction is rolled back."). The unlock SQL never reached the driver, and the advisory lock leaked until SQLAlchemy's pool recycled the session — which under the prod pool can be minutes-to-hours. Other replicas waiting on `pg_try_advisory_lock(42)` then block on the next refresh attempt with no observable cause. Fix (Option B from the brief): `finally` now calls `await conn.rollback()` (wrapped in `contextlib.suppress(Exception)` to keep it best-effort and to avoid masking the in-flight exception that brought control into the finally) BEFORE opening the unlock txn. Rollback resets the aborted-transaction state on the same `conn`, so the subsequent `async with conn.begin(): await conn.execute(text("SELECT pg_advisory_unlock(42)"))` succeeds and the session-scoped lock is released. The lock-key constant, txn ordering (ingest+collapse → embed → HNSW+truncate → tags → unlock → mark_finished), and `mark_finished()` semantics in the outermost `finally` are untouched. `from contextlib import suppress` added alongside the existing imports in `refresh.py`. New `tests/test_refresh_runner_lock_leak.py` (2 cases): (1) `test_unlock_runs_when_embed_leaves_conn_aborted` — bug-coverage red — injects an `embed` callable that flips an `aborted=True` flag on the fake conn before raising; the `_AbortableFakeConn.begin()` then raises `InvalidRequestError` while `aborted` is set (and `rollback()` clears the flag, mirroring real SA semantics), so the test passes only if the finally rollbacks-then-unlocks; asserts exactly one `pg_advisory_unlock` execution, at least one rollback, and `status.running == False`. (2) `test_unlock_runs_on_happy_path_without_extra_rollback_noise` — sanity: clean conn, ingest returns 3, exactly one unlock, runner returns 3. Tests use a dedicated `_AbortableFakeConn` (extends the existing `_FakeConn` shape from `test_ingest_refresh.py` with `aborted`, `rollbacks`, `begin_attempts`) — kept in the new file rather than mutating the shared fake to scope the new failure-mode surface to D10.9's regression test. Run RED-then-GREEN verified manually: the aborted-state test fails at `refresh.py:460` (`async with conn.begin()` → `InvalidRequestError`) without the fix, passes with it. Step 8 (Smoke) skipped — internal refresh-runner txn boundary, no public API change. Docstring on `run_locked` updated with one sentence documenting the rollback-before-unlock contract; README "First-boot tech notes" `Refresh runner txn split.` bullet extended with one phrase about lock-release independence from prior-txn state. Gate: ruff clean (only finding is the pre-existing I001 in `tests/test_color_sort.py` from `63efbd6`, out of scope per task brief); mypy clean (33 src files); pytest 559→561 pass (1 skipped, +2 new in `test_refresh_runner_lock_leak.py`).
- [x] D10.4 `_auto_refresh_if_empty` task held on `app.state` + cancelled on lifespan shutdown (§6.5 ops, README `AUTO_REFRESH_IF_EMPTY`). Symptom: `src/biblioplex/main.py` was calling `asyncio.create_task(_run())` and dropping the returned `Task`. Python's `asyncio` docs warn that the event loop only keeps a weak reference in its task-set, so a fire-and-forget task can be garbage-collected mid-flight — for a fresh-DB boot that means the (10–30 min) auto-ingest can vanish silently, leaving the `cards` table stranded. Two-sided fix in `_auto_refresh_if_empty`: (a) the created task is stored as `app.state._auto_refresh_task` so the strong ref outlives the function call; (b) `_run`'s `finally` clears the attribute when the coroutine completes (success or failure), gated by `is asyncio.current_task()` so a future re-kick in the same process doesn't accidentally wipe a fresh task ref. Lifespan teardown gains a paired branch BEFORE engine `dispose()`: `task = getattr(app.state, "_auto_refresh_task", None); if task is not None and not task.done(): task.cancel(); with suppress(asyncio.CancelledError): await task` — ordering matters because `read_engine.dispose()` would otherwise race a half-finished ingest's pool checkouts. `from contextlib import suppress` added to the existing import line. No new env var, no behavior change to `RefreshRunner.run_locked`, no touch to the `try_mark_started` / advisory-lock pair (both safety nets stay). New `tests/test_auto_refresh_lifespan.py` (4 cases) pins all four invariants: (1) empty `cards` + `auto_refresh=True` stores an `asyncio.Task` on `app.state._auto_refresh_task`; (2) non-empty `cards` leaves the attribute unset/None; (3) `auto_refresh=False` skips the probe entirely (no attr regardless of card count); (4) a hung ingest (blocked on an `asyncio.Event` that never fires) is `cancelled()` after lifespan exit and the `CancelledError` does not propagate out of the `async with app.router.lifespan_context(app):` block — the actual shutdown branch is exercised, not just inspected. Fakes follow the `tests/test_admin_refresh.py` pattern (`_FakeConn` with both `__aenter__`/`__aexit__` and a `begin()` returning self) plus an autouse monkeypatch fixture that stubs `load_model`/`assert_model_dim`/`scryfall_catalogs.fetch_keyword_set` so lifespan doesn't try to download a HF model or hit Scryfall. Step 8 (Smoke) skipped — internal lifespan wiring, no public API change. ### M1 · Ingest

- [x] D1.5 Staging + collapse — `cards_staging` table, `INSERT … SELECT DISTINCT ON (oracle_id) … ORDER BY oracle_id, released_at ASC NULLS LAST`.
- [x] D1.6 Refresh runner — `pg_advisory_lock(42)`, FastAPI `BackgroundTasks`, idempotent. Truncate staging at end.

Schema gains two columns + matching NULLS-LAST btree indexes on `cards`, and parallel raw columns on `cards_staging`. `_to_staging_row` passes `edhrec_rank` through verbatim and coerces `prices.usd` via the new `_parse_usd` helper (Decimal on clean strings, None on null/empty/garbage). Collapse SELECT replaces the raw `price_usd` with `MIN(price_usd) OVER (PARTITION BY oracle_id)` so the kept printing carries the cheapest price across the oracle group; `edhrec_rank` is per-oracle so it stays a bare column. Builds the column the D4.6 router consumer needs to resolve the open §6.1 drift entries about `order=edhrec` and a USD sort.

### M6 · Similar

- [x] D6.1 `GET /cards/:id/similar` per §6.3 — two-step query: `SELECT embedding FROM cards WHERE id=:id` then `SELECT id, raw, embedding <=> :target AS distance FROM cards WHERE id <> :id AND embedding IS NOT NULL ORDER BY embedding <=> :target LIMIT :limit`. Route registered before `/{card_id}` for path-order consistency (no UUID shadowing risk — distinct trailing segment).
- [x] D6.2 `limit` validation 1–100 default 20 + 404 on missing target / NULL embedding — `Query(default=20, ge=1, le=100)` handles bounds; the target-lookup short-circuit (no neighbour query when `scalar_one_or_none() is None`) collapses "row missing" and "embedding NULL" onto the same `not_found` envelope. Tests-only commit — behaviour was incidentally correct from D6.1, now pinned.
- [x] D6.3 List envelope + `similarity = 1 - distance` annotation — `{object:"list", total_cards:len(data), has_more:false, next_page:null, data:[...]}`. Each entry's `raw` is shallow-copied and decorated with `similarity` before send so the SQL `distance` column never leaks to clients. Drift logged at §6.3 about `total_cards` semantics.

### M3 · Search DSL

- [x] D3.1 Tokenizer — `key:value`, `"quoted"`, parens, `OR`, leading `-`, regex `/.../`, exact `!`.
- [x] D3.2 Recursive-descent parser → AST. Single file, single entrypoint `parse(q) -> Expr`.
- [x] D3.3 AST → SQLAlchemy compiler — emits one `WHERE` expression tree.
- [x] D3.4 Color set ops on `colors_eff` per §5.4 — guild/shard/wedge/4-color nicknames, `c:m`/`c:c`, numeric `c=2`.
- [x] D3.5 Mana cost multiset per §5.5 — tokenize symbols, per-face any-match for `m:`/`m=`/`m>`.
- [x] D3.6 Numeric comparators on `cmc`/`pow`/`tou`/`pt`/`loy` — non-numeric values (`*`, `X`) cast to NULL, never match.
- [x] D3.7 `is:commander` (already a column) + front-face-only `is:permanent|spell|vanilla|frenchvanilla` per §5.1 fix.
- [x] D3.8 `is:dfc|mdfc|transform|split|flip|leveler|meld|tdfc` → `layout =` mapping; `is:hybrid`/`is:phyrexian` regex on `mana_cost_search`.
- [x] D3.9 `f:`/`banned:`/`restricted:` → `legalities->>X`.
- [x] D3.10 `r:`/`rarity:` ordered comparator (`common<uncommon<rare<special<mythic<bonus`).
- [x] D3.11 `kw:`, `produces:`, `has:indicator`, `has:watermark`, `layout:`.
- [x] D3.12 `~` placeholder in `o:` substitutes top-level `name`.
- [x] D3.13 Boolean composition — implicit AND, explicit `OR`, `-`, parens.
- [x] D3.14 Regex `/.../` for `name`, `o`, `t` via Postgres `~*`.
- [x] D3.15 Unsupported-operator rejection per §5.2 → 422 with `unsupported operator: <key>` body.
- [x] §5.1 drift resolved — `is:frenchvanilla` now reads the Scryfall `/catalog/keyword-abilities`, `/catalog/keyword-actions`, `/catalog/ability-words` snapshots cached by `biblioplex.scryfall.catalogs`. Lifespan fetches once at boot; the compiler strips parenthesised reminder text and checks the rest against a regex built from the catalog union. Best-effort: any fetch failure leaves the cache empty and the legacy `[.:,]`-negation heuristic stays as fallback so search keeps working offline. Tests in `tests/test_scryfall_catalogs.py` and `tests/dsl/test_compiler_is.py`.
- [x] D3.16 `game:` operator — `raw->'games' ? X` (jsonb existence) against the kept printing's `games` array. Moved from §5.2 rejected to §5.1 supported. Latest-printing-only ceiling tracked under Spec Drift §5.1. Tests in `tests/dsl/test_compiler_game.py`; `game` removed from `tests/dsl/test_compiler_rejected.py` parametrize.
- [x] D3.17 `commander:X` operator — color-identity subset filter aliased to `id<=X` on `cards.color_identity`. Accepts only `:` (Scryfall convention); `commander=`/`<=`/`>=`/etc. raise `UnsupportedOperatorError` → 422. Nicknames (`esper`, `bant`, `wubrg`) expand via `parse_color_letters`; `commander:c`/`colorless` → `cardinality(color_identity)=0`. No implicit format-legality merge — operators stay orthogonal (matches Scryfall). Tests in `tests/dsl/test_compiler_colors.py` cover letter+nickname subset, colorless, op-rejection (`=`, `<=`), and orthogonal composition with `is:commander f:commander`.

- [x] D9.9 Resizable panels — `web/helpers.js` gains pure `clampSplit(percent, min, max)` (defaults `[15, 85]`); NaN/missing falls back to the midpoint of the bounds so a garbage `localStorage` value or a runaway drag can never fully collapse a pane. `web/index.html` inserts a 6px `<div id="splitter" role="separator" aria-orientation="vertical" tabindex="0">` between `#results` and `#detail` — placed as a grid track between the two panes so the splitter participates in layout instead of needing absolute positioning. `web/style.css` `main`'s `grid-template-columns` becomes `minmax(0, var(--split, 33%)) 6px minmax(0, 1fr)` (3 tracks: results, splitter, detail). `#splitter` carries `cursor: col-resize`, `touch-action: none` (so pointer-down doesn't trigger mobile pan-scroll), `user-select: none` (no text selection mid-drag), and `var(--border)` background swapped to `var(--accent)` on `:hover` and `:focus-visible`. Mobile breakpoint (`@media (max-width: 768px)`) sets `#splitter { display: none }` and adds a zero-height middle row (`grid-template-rows: 40vh 0 60vh`) so the splitter track collapses cleanly when the vertical stack takes over — the existing stacked layout is preserved. `web/app.js.template` wires pointer-drag: `pointerdown` captures the pointer on the splitter; `pointermove` computes `((e.clientX - mainRect.left) / mainRect.width) * 100` and pushes it through `clampSplit` before setting `mainEl.style.setProperty("--split", "${pct}%")` (drives the CSS variable directly — no per-frame DOM thrash); `pointerup` releases capture and persists the applied clamped value to `localStorage["biblioplex.split"]`. On boot, the persisted value (if any) is re-applied through the same `_applySplit` helper so any out-of-range stored value is silently re-clamped. Pointer events (not mouse) chosen so touch + pen + mouse all work via one code path; `setPointerCapture` keeps the drag responsive even when the cursor leaves the splitter element. Read- helper module-private `_applySplit(percent)` returns the clamped value so the persist path always stores what was actually rendered (avoids drift if a future code path mutates the bounds). 8 new inline-assertion cases in `web/test_helpers.html` under "clampSplit (D9.9)": in-range pass-through, below-min clamp, above-max clamp, NaN → midpoint, undefined → midpoint, numeric-string coercion, custom bounds override, boundary value stays. Serializer drops keys equal to the default so a fresh boot renders as a bare path — no `?q=&order=name&dir=auto&page=1` noise. `urlStateFromSearch` is forgiving on bad `page=` input (non-numeric, ≤0) and falls back to 1 so a hand-typed URL can't crash the boot. `web/app.js.template` wires three call sites: (1) `_bootFromUrl()` on first paint hydrates the inputs + `currentPage` + `selectedId` from the URL and auto-selects the linked card after the search resolves — URL `q` wins over `DEFAULT_Q` because explicit user intent (shared link, refresh, bookmarked deep link) trumps the deploy-config default; (2) `_syncUrl()` is called from `search()` and `selectCard()` to keep the URL bar mirrored to the current state without spamming `replaceState` (target-vs-current diff guard skips no-op writes); (3) `popstate` listener re-hydrates inputs + re-runs the search + re-selects when the user navigates back/forward. `search()` gained an optional `page` parameter (defaults to 1, clamped to ≥1) so the URL state can drive paginated loads — the chip-append path keeps the existing 1-arg call site working by resetting to `page=1` on every form submit (a new query invalidates the old page index). ### M11 · Fuzzy search

Default order selection stays `name` (still the first non-relevance option, matches `_URL_DEFAULTS.order` in `web/helpers.js`), so a cold-boot load keeps the existing alphabetical behaviour and `urlStateToSearch` continues to drop `order=` from the URL on a default boot. URL state round-trips through the existing `urlStateFromSearch`/`urlStateToSearch` pair (D9.7) with no helper changes needed — `relevance` is non-default so it serialises as `?order=relevance` automatically and shareable links work out of the box. `_sortField(order)` map needs no entry (relevance has no projected field to surface, mirroring the `name`/`cmc`/`rarity` no-op branches). - [x] D11.1 Backend trigram fuzzy name-match + `order=relevance` ranking. Symptom-→-fix: bare-word and quoted-phrase searches compiled to `name_search ILIKE %X%` only (`src/biblioplex/dsl/compiler.py:_name_substring`), so typos like `invason` silently returned zero rows even though `pg_trgm` was installed and the `cards_name_trgm` GIN index (`gin_trgm_ops` on `name_search`, `schema.sql:115-116`) was already present and idle. Fix at three layers. (1) `_name_substring` now ORs the literal ILIKE with `cards.name_search.op("%>")(value)` — `%>` is `word_similarity(arg, name_search) >= pg_trgm.word_similarity_threshold`, GIN-indexable, and chosen over plain `%` because card names are long relative to query fragments (whole-string similarity of `invason` vs `Invasion of Ikoria` is below any reasonable threshold; `word_similarity` measures the best matching substring extent instead). Threshold lives as the new module-level constant `NAME_WORD_SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD = 0.4` in compiler.py, mirroring `FUZZY_SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD` in `routers/cards.py:127` so `/cards/named?fuzzy=` and the search-side OR share one knob. (2) `src/biblioplex/db.py` `make_read_engine` extends the libpq `connect_args["options"]` string with a second `-c pg_trgm.word_similarity_threshold=0.4` switch alongside the existing `-c statement_timeout=…`, so every read-pool connection has the GUC set at session start — without that, the GIN index would not be eligible for the `%>` clause because Postgres compares against the session-local threshold, not the constant in the predicate. Refresh-pool engine deliberately not touched (ingest doesn't search). (3) New `compile_rank(ast, cards) -> ColumnElement[float] | None` (separate function, NOT a return-type change on `compile_ast` — the latter would have broken every caller and every test, so a parallel function was the lazy-correct choice) walks the AST collecting bare-word + unkeyed-quoted-phrase + `name:` KV values via the new `_extract_bare_terms` helper; values are joined by space and passed to `word_similarity(:joined, name_search)`. Negated subtrees (`-elf`) are skipped — a user excluding a name doesn't want results ranked by similarity to it. Filter-only queries (`t:legendary`) return `None` so the router can fall back to a deterministic sort without a 500. Router (`src/biblioplex/routers/cards.py`) gains `SearchOrder.relevance`, `_AUTO_DIR[relevance] = desc`, and `_order_expression(order, rank=None)` (new optional second arg) returns `rank if rank is not None else _CARDS.c.name_sort_key` for the relevance branch. The search-endpoint call site computes `rank` only when `order is SearchOrder.relevance` (avoiding the AST walk for every other order), and when `order=relevance` but `rank is None` it resolves `dir=auto` against `SearchOrder.name` instead of `SearchOrder.relevance` so the fallback uses `asc` (matching `order=name`) rather than `desc`. Tests-first per process step 4: extended `tests/dsl/test_compiler.py` to assert both `ILIKE` AND `name_search %%>` (psycopg paramstyle doubles `%` in rendered SQL — substring match works on the doubled form) appear in the compiled WHERE for bare-word + `name:` KV; new `tests/dsl/test_compiler_relevance.py` (6 cases) pins the `compile_rank` contract — bare word produces non-None rank containing `word_similarity`, filter-only returns None, mixed query joins only bare terms (`legendary` does NOT leak into the rank when `q=invason t:legendary`), `name:` KV contributes, quoted phrase contributes, multiple bare words concat-join; extended `tests/test_cards_search_d4_2.py` — added `relevance` to the secondary-tie-break loop, added `("relevance", "desc")` to the `dir=auto` parametrize matrix, added two new cases asserting the SQL shape for `order=relevance` (word_similarity in primary clause, ` desc` direction) and the filter-only fallback (`name_sort_key asc`, no `word_similarity`). Smoke (`scripts/smoke.sh`) extended with step 10/10: `GET /cards/search?q=invason&order=relevance` asserts `total_cards > 0`. Spec edits in the same commit per process.md "spec is source of truth" rule (drift is for spec silence, not author-requested feature additions): `tech_spec.md` §5.1 bare-word row gains the `OR name_search %> X` clause with the threshold note; §6.1 order enum gains `relevance` with the per-order semantics + filter-only fallback. README "Search DSL" section gained one `invason` worked-example line + a one-paragraph fuzzy/relevance note; `/cards/search` param table updated to list `relevance` and its dir-auto behavior. Step 8 (Smoke) required because `SearchOrder` enum gains a public value — extended `scripts/smoke.sh` accordingly. Step 10 (Docs): docstring on `_name_substring` explains the OR shape + GUC contract; `compile_rank` carries its own contract docstring covering AST nodes contributing, negation handling, and why `word_similarity` function (rather than the indexed `%>` operator) is used in the ORDER BY position; `SearchOrder.relevance` and `_order_expression`'s relevance branch are documented in their respective docstrings. Gate: ruff clean for files in scope (only finding is the pre-existing I001 in `tests/test_color_sort.py` from `63efbd6`, out of scope per D10.11 backlog); mypy clean (33 src files); pytest 585 → 594 pass (1 skipped, +9 new — +6 in `test_compiler_relevance.py`, +2 in `test_cards_search_d4_2.py`, +1 from the `relevance` parametrize row in `test_dir_auto_per_order_mode`).

- [x] D10.10 Functional index `lower(name)` + trgm GIN on `name_sort_key` (§6.4). Symptom: `src/biblioplex/routers/cards.py:566–574` ran `SELECT raw FROM cards WHERE LOWER(name) = LOWER(:name)` (exact mode) against ~30k rows with no functional index on `lower(name)` in `schema.sql`, forcing a seq-scan on every `/cards/named?exact=` request; the fuzzy mode `SELECT raw FROM cards WHERE similarity(name_sort_key, :name) >= 0.4 ORDER BY similarity(name_sort_key, :name) DESC LIMIT 1` also seq-scanned because `name_sort_key` only had the plain btree `cards_name_sort_key_idx` (which equality / `ORDER BY` use but `pg_trgm`'s `similarity()` cannot). Neither is a wrong-answer bug; both are scalability cliffs at the ingest-grown row count. Fix: two new `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` statements appended to the §3.2 index block in `schema.sql` right after `cards_name_sort_key_idx` — (1) `cards_name_lower_idx ON cards (lower(name))` backs the exact-mode functional equality; (2) `cards_name_sort_key_trgm ON cards USING gin (name_sort_key gin_trgm_ops)` backs the fuzzy-mode `similarity(name_sort_key, ...)` lookup. The existing `cards_name_sort_key_idx` btree is intentionally preserved (regression-pinned by the test) — both can coexist because they serve disjoint access paths: btree for equality + `ORDER BY name_sort_key` (used by `order=name` in `/cards/search`, D4.2), GIN for trgm similarity. No drop, no alter, no Alembic — schema.sql remains the single source of truth per the existing architecture. **Bootstrap-reapply caveat**: `schema.sql` is mounted into `/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/` and only auto-runs on a fresh data volume (per README "First-boot tech notes"), so the two new indexes land automatically on fresh DBs (`docker compose down -v && docker compose up --build`) AND can be applied to an existing populated DB without the destructive reset by manually running just the two `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS …` statements — the `IF NOT EXISTS` guard makes that fragment-reapply safe and a no-op on a DB that already has them. Tests: new `tests/test_schema_indexes.py` (6 cases) parses `schema.sql` as plain text — NO live Postgres, NO `EXPLAIN`. The schema-text approach was chosen deliberately: a live-DB check would require standing up Postgres in the test runner (adding `testcontainers` or a `pytest-docker` fixture, ~seconds of per-suite overhead) for what is purely a declaration that the file ships the two statements, and the existing test suite has no precedent for spinning up Postgres mid-run (compiler tests assert SQL text, ingest tests use `_FakeConn`). Cases: (1) `test_cards_name_lower_idx_declared` — substring presence; (2) `test_cards_name_lower_idx_shape` — regex pinning `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS cards_name_lower_idx ON cards (lower(name));` with whitespace tolerance; (3) `test_cards_name_sort_key_trgm_declared` — substring presence; (4) `test_cards_name_sort_key_trgm_shape` — regex pinning `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS cards_name_sort_key_trgm ON cards USING gin (name_sort_key gin_trgm_ops);`; (5) `test_existing_name_sort_key_btree_preserved` — regression guard the btree `cards_name_sort_key_idx ON cards (name_sort_key)` still ships (so a future cleanup pass doesn't accidentally drop the coexisting btree); (6) `test_new_indexes_use_if_not_exists` — pins the `IF NOT EXISTS` guard on both new statements so the fragment-reapply path stays safe. Module-scoped `schema_text` fixture reads `schema.sql` once. Step 8 (Smoke) skipped — schema-only change ships in `schema.sql` which only applies on fresh DB bootstrap; the existing compose stack would not pick up the new indexes without `docker compose down -v`, so `scripts/smoke.sh` (which runs against the live stack) would not exercise the fix path. README "First-boot tech notes" → "Schema bootstrap is single-pass" bullet's parenthetical list of schema-change examples extended to call out D10.10's two indexes alongside the D1.9 and oracle-tag examples. No `docs/` change; no `tech_spec.md` edit (out of scope per task brief). Gate: ruff clean for files in scope (the only finding is the pre-existing I001 in `tests/test_color_sort.py` from `63efbd6`, out of scope per task brief); mypy clean (33 src files); pytest 579 → 585 pass (1 skipped, +6 new in `test_schema_indexes.py`).
- [x] D10.7 `_vec_to_literal` clamps non-finite components to `0.0` (§4.2 step 5, §8.3). Symptom: `src/biblioplex/embed/batch.py:53–58` serialized each component via `repr(float(x))`; `repr(float('inf'))` → `'inf'` and `repr(float('nan'))` → `'nan'`, both of which pgvector's `CAST(:vec AS vector)` rejects. One rogue component in any encoded vector would abort the entire `embed_pending` page mid-`executemany`, strand the in-progress UPDATE, and (combined with D10.9's lock-release contract) require the next refresh to re-do every page that hadn't yet committed. SentenceTransformer rarely emits non-finites on well-formed inputs — empty `embed_source` strings hash to a finite all-zero(-ish) vector — but custom HF models or pathological tokenization can blow up, and the failure mode is "whole refresh dies" rather than "one bad row gets a junk vector". Defense-in-depth choice: clamp instead of skip. `0.0` is the lowest-magnitude finite value pgvector accepts; the zero vector has undefined cosine similarity to any other vector but it round-trips through `CAST(... AS vector)` cleanly and the rogue row stays NULL-free, so the next ranked retrieval just won't surface it. Skipping the row was non-viable because the `executemany` payload pairs `id`→`vec` row-by-row (one vector per `cards` row by contract); a missing entry would either desync the zip or leave `embedding IS NULL`, which would re-trigger the same encode on the next refresh and re-hit the same bug. Fix: new module-private `_finite_or_zero(x: float) -> float` (returns `x` when `math.isfinite(x)`, else `0.0`) is called inside the existing comprehension before `repr`. `import math` added alongside the existing imports. No log line per clamped component — some encoders could emit thousands of non-finites in one degenerate vector and per-component log spam would drown out the rest of the refresh; silent clamp is the right call for D10.7. No change to `embed_pending` itself, no change to `SELECT_PENDING_SQL` / `UPDATE_EMBEDDING_SQL`, no public API delta. `_vec_to_literal`'s docstring gains one sentence calling out the clamp + the rationale. Tests: `tests/test_embed_batch.py` gains 6 cases (`+inf` → 0.0 at the right position with round-trip parse; `-inf` ditto; `nan` ditto; all-finite regression guard against the original `repr(float(x))` output verbatim; numpy-array-like input via a `tolist()`-only stub still works AND clamps mixed `inf`/`nan`; a final mixed-vector belt-and-suspenders case asserting the rendered literal contains no `inf`/`nan`/`Infinity`/`NaN` substring case-insensitive). Two small helpers in the test file (`_parse_literal`, `_has_nonfinite_token`) keep each assertion narrow. Red-then-green verified manually: before the fix, the `+inf` case fails at the first assertion with `[0.1,inf,0.3]`; after the fix it parses to `[0.1, 0.0, 0.3]`. Step 8 (Smoke) skipped — internal vector serialization, no public API change. Gate: ruff clean (only finding is the pre-existing I001 in `tests/test_color_sort.py` from `63efbd6`, out of scope per task brief); mypy clean (33 src files); pytest 561 → 567 pass (1 skipped, +6 new in `test_embed_batch.py`).
- [x] D10.2 `c=COLORS` set-equality via WUBRG-sorted `colors_eff` (§5.4 drift resolved). Symptom: a multi-face / DFC card whose top-level `colors` is null had `colors_eff` ingested in face order (e.g. `["U","R","W"]`) and Postgres array `=` is ORDERED, so `c=WUR` (which compiled to `colors_eff = ARRAY['R','U','W']` via the compiler's alphabetical `sorted(parse_color_letters(...))`) silently missed it. Fix lives at ingest: `src/biblioplex/ingest/flatten.py:_colors_eff` now dedupes, filters to `{W,U,B,R,G}` (defensive — Scryfall has never emitted other letters but the spec gives no contract), and sorts in canonical WUBRG order on BOTH branches (`if top is not None` and the face-union branch). The §5.4 drift resolution wording said "compiler stays as-is", but the compiler actually sorted alphabetically (e.g. `sorted({"W","U","R"})` → `["R","U","W"]`), so a WUBRG-sorted ingest column would STILL not match the compiler's alphabetically-sorted query literal. Resolved by making both sides agree on WUBRG: `src/biblioplex/dsl/compiler.py:_compile_color` now uses `sorted(parse_color_letters(value), key=_WUBRG_INDEX.__getitem__)` (with a `.get(c, len(_WUBRG_INDEX))` fallback so non-WUBRG letters like `C` mixed via `c:CW` sort to the end deterministically without `KeyError` — ingest never stores `C` in `colors_eff` so such queries match nothing, same as today). Module-level `_WUBRG_INDEX = {c: i for i, c in enumerate("WUBRG")}` on each side, mirrored docstring comments calling out the symmetric-sort contract. No schema/migration required — `colors_eff` is recomputed at every `POST /admin/refresh` and stored in `cards`; the next refresh rewrites every row with WUBRG-sorted values. Tests: `tests/test_ingest_flatten.py` gains 5 cases (top-level WUBRG sort, face-union WUBRG sort, empty list, cross-face dedup-then-sort, non-WUBRG letter filter); `tests/dsl/test_compiler_colors.py` gains 2 cases asserting the rendered query-side literal is in WUBRG order (`ARRAY['W','U','R']` for `c=WUR`, `ARRAY['W','U','B','R','G']` for `c=wubrg`) — pinned at the SQL-literal layer because there is no DB fixture for compiler tests. Step 8 (Smoke) skipped — internal flatten + compiler contract, no public API change. 548 → 555 tests pass (1 skipped, +7 new). ruff clean for files in scope; mypy clean. Pre-existing I001 in `tests/test_color_sort.py` left alone (out of scope per task brief).
- [x] D10.1 CORS middleware wired to `Settings.cors_origins` (§10, README "Agent-facing notes"). `src/biblioplex/main.py` `create_app` now imports `fastapi.middleware.cors.CORSMiddleware` and installs it immediately after the settings handshake and before the engines/routers wire up. The middleware reads `settings.cors_origins` and CSV-splits with `[o.strip() for o in resolved.cors_origins.split(",") if o.strip()]` so leading/trailing whitespace per entry is forgiving; the `"*"` default short-circuits to `["*"]` because the comprehension yields `["*"]` for a single-entry CSV and an empty list (no non-empty entries) falls back to `["*"]` via `origins or ["*"]`. `allow_methods=["*"]` and `allow_headers=["*"]` because the README's "Agent-facing notes" block explicitly disclaims an auth surface — there is nothing to protect by narrowing the preflight allowlist. No new env var (`CORS_ORIGINS` was already declared in §10 and `config.py:30`); no schema/migration; no behaviour change to any router. New `tests/test_cors.py` (4 cases): wildcard GET echoes `*` on a cross-origin request, wildcard OPTIONS preflight returns 200 with `access-control-allow-methods`, CSV allowlist `https://a.com,https://b.com` echoes `https://a.com` for an allowed origin and omits the allow-origin header for `https://c.com`. Tests use the same `Settings.model_construct(...)` factory + `ASGITransport` pattern as `tests/test_health.py` with the `get_read_engine` dep override so /health serves 200 without a real DB. README's existing "CORS open by default" line is now truthful — no edit needed. Boot guard skips the auto-search when landing directly on `?tab=deck` to spare an unused round-trip. ### Spec Drift / §6.1

- [x] §6.1 — `order=color` now ships the canonical WUBRG-cycle position (resolved via spec-drift sweep, user picked "canonical WUBRG cycle" over cardinality / "unspecified"). New `src/biblioplex/ingest/color_sort.py` encodes a 32-entry lookup `color_sort_key(colors: list[str] | None) -> int`: 0 colorless → 1–5 mono (W,U,B,R,G) → 6–10 2c allied (WU,UB,BR,RG,GW) → 11–15 2c enemy (WB,BG,UR,RW,UG) → 16–20 3c shards (WUB,UBR,BRG,RGW,GWU) → 21–25 3c wedges (WBG,URW,BGU,RWB,GUR) → 26–30 4c (UBRG,WBRG,WURG,WUBG,WUBR — missing W/U/B/R/G in WUBRG order) → 31 5c WUBRG. Input is WUBRG-sorted + deduped + unknown-letter-dropped before lookup so any color array shape normalizes to one key; empty / fully-unrecognized → 0. Schema gains `cards.color_sort_key int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` + `cards_color_sort_key_idx` btree and `cards_staging.color_sort_key int`; `_to_staging_row` computes it from `flat["colors_eff"]` (per-printing identical, DISTINCT ON kept value is fine). `_order_expression` routes `order=color` to `cards.color_sort_key` (replacing the prior `cardinality(colors_eff)` shipped behavior); `_AUTO_DIR` keeps `asc` so colorless surfaces first. Tests in `tests/test_color_sort.py` pin every cycle position + dedup/permutation/unknown-letter invariants, `tests/test_ingest_staging_row.py` covers passthrough, `tests/test_cards_search_d4_2.py` routing updated. 525/525 pass.

Resolved by D1.9 (added `cards.edhrec_rank` int + index NULLS LAST, populated from Scryfall's `edhrec_rank` field) and D4.6 (wired `_order_expression` to the column, with `dir=auto` defaulting to `asc` so rank 1 = most popular surfaces first, matching Scryfall's `order=edhrec` convention).

FROM cards_staging, unnest(coalesce(games, '{}'::text[])) AS g GROUP BY oracle_id)` CTE that the main collapse SELECT `LEFT JOIN`s on `oracle_id`. Schema gains `cards.games_eff text[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` + `cards_games_eff_gin` GIN index, and `cards_staging.games text[]` (per-printing raw). `_to_staging_row` passes `games` through verbatim (Scryfall ships it as a top-level text list). `_cards_column()` maps the staging `games` name to the cards `games_eff` name in the INSERT column list + ON CONFLICT UPDATE pairs. `_compile_game` switches from `raw->'games' ? X` (kept-printing only) to `games_eff @> ARRAY[X]::text[]` (cross-printing union, GIN-indexed). Fixes the drift where a paper-first-then-Arena card would have missed `game:arena`. Tests: `tests/dsl/test_compiler_game.py` (games_eff containment, no regression to raw->'games'), `tests/test_collapse_sql.py` (CTE shape, LEFT JOIN, games_eff projection + update pair), `tests/test_ingest_collapse.py` (staging columns list + ON CONFLICT loop via `_cards_column`), `tests/test_ingest_staging_row.py` (games passthrough + null/empty variants). 485/485 pass.

### Spec Drift / §3.1

- [x] §3.1 — `is_commander` derivation confirmed in spec-drift sweep. Top-level `type_line` is the right field — Scryfall puts the front-face type_line at top level for DFC/transform/MDFC/adventure/meld, and commander legality is always determined by the front face (no back-face-only commander exists). Split/flip cards are never legendary creatures, so face-flatten buys nothing. Case-insensitive substring check is safe given Scryfall's invariant `[Supertypes] [Types] — [Subtypes]` formatting: "Legendary" and "Creature" only appear as full tokens. The `can be your commander` arm reads `oracle_search` (flattened) so back-face planeswalker / partner / background text matches too. No code change.

### Bugs

- [x] Bug.30 `ingest/tags.py:ingest_oracle_tags` — TRUNCATE swapped for DELETE inside the refresh txn. TRUNCATE takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE and holds it until commit; on a 543k-row rebuild the batched INSERT loop exceeded the read pool's 5s `statement_timeout` (`db.py:33`), and every reader touching `oracle_tags` (`/cards/{id}/similar` target-row subquery in `_TARGET_ROW_SQL`, `/cards/{id}/tags` LEFT JOIN, DSL `otag:` EXISTS subqueries, `/decks/bracket` `SIGNAL_TAG_SLUGS` aggregate SQL, `/decks/synergy` tag_pairs join) 500'd with `psycopg.errors.QueryCanceled: canceling statement due to statement timeout`. DELETE takes ROW EXCLUSIVE — does not conflict with reader ACCESS SHARE — so readers keep hitting the pre-refresh snapshot under MVCC for the whole rebuild window, then see the new set atomically at commit. Cost: ~543k dead tuples per refresh vs zero; autovacuum handles it. Skipped rename-swap dance / CONCURRENTLY REFRESH pattern — add when dead-tuple bloat measurably hurts. `_TRUNCATE_SQL` renamed to `_CLEAR_SQL` at the same time; module docstring step 6 rewritten to name the lock discipline. Pin: `test_bug30_clear_uses_delete_not_truncate` in `tests/test_ingest_tags.py` asserts `_CLEAR_SQL` starts with `DELETE FROM ORACLE_TAGS` and contains no `TRUNCATE`. Gate: ruff clean on touched files (3 pre-existing errors unchanged elsewhere), mypy clean on `ingest/tags.py`, pytest 1162 → 1163 pass (+1 new; 2 pre-existing D15.33-stale `set:` unsupported_operator failures unchanged).

- [x] Bug.28 `parse_decklist` round-trip fidelity on names beginning with `x` / `X`. Bug.15's `1xSol Ring` fix widened the qty regex's post-`[xX]?` gate to accept an alphabetic look-ahead in place of `\s+`, which let `"1 XA"` re-parse as `qty=1, name="A"` — the `X` was gobbled as a marker because the alphabetic look-ahead applied unconditionally, ignoring whether `\s*` had already consumed whitespace between the digits and the `[xX]`. Hypothesis property `test_idempotent_on_str_roundtrip` shrank to `"XA"` at max_examples=2000 (default 100 misses it). Fix in `src/biblioplex/decklist/parser.py`: split the single `[xX]?(?:\s+|(?=[A-Za-z]))` clause into three explicit alternation branches — `\s*[xX]\s+` (space-separated marker, either side), `[xX](?=[A-Za-z])` (Bug.15 cubeware, marker directly glued to digits — no `\s*` in front), and `\s+` (no marker). The cubeware branch's requirement that the marker sit *immediately* after the digits means a bare `X` at the start of a name that follows a space can never be misread as the marker: the `\s+` branch fires first and captures the space, leaving `X…` at the name position. Two new pins in `tests/test_decklist_parser.py`: `test_bug28_bare_x_prefix_is_name_not_marker` (6 params — `"1 XA"`, `"1 Xantcha, Sleeper Agent"`, `"1 Xenagos, the Reveler"`, `"2 xylem"`, `"XA"`, `"Xantcha, Sleeper Agent"`) and `test_bug28_round_trip_fixed_point` (8 params — the shrunk Hypothesis case plus every Bug.15 cubeware seed to prove no regression). Gate: ruff clean on touched files (3 pre-existing errors unchanged), mypy clean (46 src files), pytest 1043 → 1057 pass (+14 new).

- [x] Bug.29 Web UI dead on load — `Uncaught SyntaxError: redeclaration of const _renderLegality` at `app.js:2231`, previously declared at line 334. Two unrelated helpers collided under one name: line 334 (`const _renderLegality = (fmt, status) => ...`, the per-format Scryfall legality badge in the card detail pane, D-legacy) and line 2231 (`function _renderLegality(payload)`, the deck legality violations panel introduced by D14.7). JS parse failure took out the entire `app.js` — every button dead on load, refresh button included (that was the reported symptom). Renamed the D14.7 helper to `_renderDeckLegality` and updated its sole call site at line 2206. The card-detail helper keeps the original name — it was there first. Web-only; envsubst bakes `app.js.template` into `app.js` at container start, so operators pick up the fix on `make web-reload`. No new test — repo has no JS parse / lint gate today, so a `node --check` pass on the rendered `app.js` would be the smallest correct guard. Filed as follow-up scope. Gate: n/a (no Python touched).

- [x] Bug.27 `text(":X::TYPE")` bindparam never substitutes — SQLAlchemy `text()` treats `::` as an escaped colon and drops the preceding `:X` before it. Comment at `ingest/combos.py:_INSERT_SQL` already documented the workaround (use `CAST(:X AS TYPE)`) but six later sites violated it, and no test caught the drift because every existing pin uses a fake `conn.execute` that captures `str(stmt)` without invoking sqla compile. Live-failing site was `ingest/combos.py:resolve_names_to_oracle_ids` — refresh combos stage crashed with `psycopg.errors.SyntaxError: syntax error at or near ":"` because `:names::text[]` reached Postgres literally. Fixed six sites: `ingest/combos.py:_RESOLVE_NAMES_SQL` (new module-level constant, extraction mirrors `_INSERT_SQL`), `decklist/resolve.py` exact/fuzzy/`_RESOLVE_AND_FETCH_SQL` (three sites), `routers/decks.py:decklist_manabase` nonbasic selector (three casts in one statement). All swapped `:X::TYPE` → `CAST(:X AS TYPE)`. New pin file `tests/test_bug27_bindparams_parse.py` — asserts `text(sql)._bindparams` contains the expected names for `_RESOLVE_NAMES_SQL`, `_RESOLVE_AND_FETCH_SQL`, plus three inline call-site SQL literals, and a negative pin that the pre-fix `:X::TYPE` shape really did skip the bind. Gate: ruff clean (3 pre-existing errors unchanged), mypy clean, pytest 910 → 916 pass (+6 new).

### M15 · Quality wins

- [x] D15.29 `?fields=` alias for `?projection=` on every endpoint that accepts a projection. FastAPI-side we add a sibling `fields: str | None = Query(...)` param next to the existing `projection` in each of the six touched handlers (`/cards/search`, `/cards/named`, `/cards/{card_id}/similar`, `/cards/{card_id}/substitutes`, `/cards/random`, `/cards/{card_id}`, plus `/commanders/suggest`). One coalesce line inside the body (`projection = fields if fields is not None else projection`) hands the string to the same `_apply_projection` / `parse_projection` chain, so `?projection=` stays wire-compat and `?fields=` behaves identically. `projection` param carries `include_in_schema=False` so the OpenAPI (and by extension the MCP tool-card) surfaces only the shorter `fields` name. tech_spec.md §7 gains a one-line note. Pins in new `tests/test_cards_fields_alias.py` (6 tests: `/cards/:id` fields matches projection, fields wins when both supplied, `/cards/search` fields matches projection row-for-row, `/cards/search` projection still supported for wire-compat, OpenAPI surfaces `fields` and hides `projection` on `/cards/:id` and `/cards/search`).
