## Archived 2026-08-20 — M19 deck storage (D19.1-D19.31) + remaining M14..M18

Second archive pass. Rows moved from `todo.md` `## Done` + `Spec Drift / M19`. Covers the stored-deck foundation: schema and version history, the section-aware decklist parse and its importers, folders, the derived `deck_cards` index and its refresh stage, inventory coverage, missing-card cost and deck value. The git-like branch series that builds on all of it (D19.32-D19.41) stays in `todo.md` as current context and archives next pass.

## Done

### Spec Drift / M19

- [x] M19 §6.7 — the spec describes the decklist line grammar but is silent on section headings, so D19.2 had to invent the vocabulary and the semantics of three headings that appear in NO reference export.
  - Assumed: `Companion` folds into `sideboard`; `Maybeboard` and `Tokens` are dropped entirely; `About` yields the deck name and is otherwise discarded; `Commander` / `Deck` / `Main` / `Mainboard` / `Sideboard` map as named. Headings match case-insensitively with an optional trailing colon, and ONLY from the fixed allowlist.
  - Reason: a companion occupies a sideboard slot, so it is part of the deck and dropping it would lose a card; a maybeboard and a token list are explicitly not part of the deck, so keeping them would inflate it. The allowlist (rather than a heuristic) is forced: a heading and a quantity-less card name have identical shape, and `Sol Ring` alone on a line is legal input per §6.7.
  - **Resolved 2026-08-18 by author decision, shipped as D19.10.** `Companion` is NOT sideboard — it becomes its own `DeckSections` bucket and its own optional `decks.companion` column (D19.11), mirroring `commander`. `Maybeboard` and `Tokens` confirmed dropped, as assumed. New third ruling not covered by the original entry: commander and companion entries appear LITERALLY in `main` as well as their own list, so `main` is the full card pool — which makes `DeckSections` deliberately not a partition, documented at length in the class docstring and pinned by `test_sections_are_not_a_partition`. The allowlist mechanism and the `About` / colon rules stand unchanged.

### M19 · Deck storage

- [x] D19.1 `decks` + `deck_versions` schema. Two tables plus one index, appended to `schema.sql` with `IF NOT EXISTS` so running deployments apply them in place (recipe in this commit body). `decks` is identity + user-owned metadata only — `name`, `format`, `commander`, `notes`, `deckbox_url` / `moxfield_url` / `archidekt_url`, timestamps. `deck_versions` is append-only history — `deck_id` FK `ON DELETE CASCADE`, `version` (`CHECK >= 1`), `decklist` canonical text, nullable `raw_content bytea`, `filename`, `source` CHECK'd to `upload|paste|url|edit|restore`, `size_bytes` and the three outcome counts, `UNIQUE (deck_id, version)`, and `deck_versions_deck_idx ON (deck_id, version DESC)` serving both reads that matter (latest-version LIMIT 1, and the newest-first listing).

  **There is no `decks.decklist` column, deliberately** — current content IS the highest-numbered version. A `decks.decklist` would be a cache of `deck_versions.decklist` with nothing keeping the two honest, so any write that updated one and not the other would silently detach a deck from its own history. Content is stored as decklist TEXT rather than resolved `(oracle_id, qty)` rows because every `/decks/*` endpoint already consumes a decklist string: text needs no resolution at write time, no re-serialisation at read time, and cannot rot when a refresh changes what a name resolves to. Priced cost: cross-deck questions ("which decks run Sol Ring") are a text scan; the upgrade path is a DERIVED `deck_cards` index rebuilt on write, not a change of source of truth. `commander` is an override rather than a cache — normally parsed from the versioned decklist's `Commander` section, but three of the nine reference exports in `data/example_deck_exports/` (every Deckbox one) carry no such section, so without the column those decks could never drive `/decks/legality` or `/decks/bracket`; effective value is `COALESCE(decks.commander, <parsed from latest version>)`. The three service URLs are three nullable columns rather than a `deck_links(deck_id, service, url)` table — the set is fixed at three and does not grow (same call as `inventory_sync_settings.deckbox_url`). `raw_content` is nullable because `paste`/`edit`/`restore` versions have no source file, and is `bytea` for the same reason as `uploads` (D18.17): a download must return the exact CSV/HTML that was imported, which the canonicalised text cannot reproduce, and a column needs no volume mount, no path config and no separate backup story.

  Verified against the LIVE PG 17 stack, not just the regex pins: the whole D19.1 block was extracted and executed inside `BEGIN; … ROLLBACK;` via `docker compose exec db psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1` — `CREATE TABLE` ×2 + `CREATE INDEX` clean, nothing left behind. Pins: new `tests/test_decks_schema.py` (8) — both tables declared with their columns, CASCADE FK, `raw_content bytea` present AND confirmed not `NOT NULL`, the `source` CHECK set, `UNIQUE (deck_id, version)`, the index with its `DESC`, and an explicit assertion that `decks` has NO `decklist` column so the two-sources-of-truth mistake cannot be reintroduced later. Gate: 1318 passed, 1 skipped.


- [x] D19.2 `decklist/sections.py` — section-aware decklist parse. New `DeckSections(name, commander, main, sideboard)` frozen dataclass + `parse_sections(text)`. **Wraps `parse_decklist` rather than replacing it** — this layer only decides which bucket a line belongs to and then hands each bucket's lines to the existing parser, so the line grammar, the quantity rules and Bug.33's round-trip property all stay in one place and every existing `/decks/*` caller is untouched. Lines before any heading go to `main`, which is what makes the heading-less exports (plain Moxfield, MTGO) work with zero special-casing.

  Headings are recognised ONLY from a fixed allowlist, and that is forced rather than conservative: a heading and a quantity-less card name have identical shape, so `Sol Ring` alone on a line — legal input per §6.7 — must stay a card. Consequence, pinned: a line carrying a quantity can never match a heading (`1 Sideboard` is the card). Matching is case-insensitive with an optional trailing colon; the colon is load-bearing, not cosmetic. `commander` is a LIST, not a string, because two-commander decks (partner / background / friends-forever) are legal and collapsing them here would silently drop one.

  Measured result on the reference exports: the six phantom cards on clean HEAD are gone (`archidekt/colorless_arsenal.txt` → `Commander`, `Mainboard`; `moxfield/big-boys-arena.txt` → `About`, `Name Big Boys`, `Commander`, `Deck`), and `deckbox/iroh-gift.txt` now reads 81 main + 19 sideboard = 100 cards, agreeing EXACTLY with the CSV sibling's `Section` column (89 rows, main 81/81, sideboard 8/19) — the whole prior 101-vs-100 discrepancy was the literal `Sideboard:` line being counted as a card. Arena's `About` block yields the deck name (`Big Boys`) and is otherwise discarded; `Name …` is scoped to that block on purpose, since outside it the word is just a card prefix (`Name Sticker Goblin`).

  Ground truth for the fixture pins was measured directly off the files BEFORE the module existed, not read back out of it. Counts are pinned per file and explicitly never asserted equal across files: the Moxfield exports of the same deck genuinely disagree (77 names / 114 cards plain vs 50 / 87 in the Arena `Deck` section plus its 1 commander = 88), because the Arena export drops what is not Arena-legal — an equality assertion there would have looked like a parser bug forever. Drift filed (§6.7 silent on section vocabulary; `Companion` → sideboard corrects the Pending row's `main`). Pins: new `tests/test_decklist_sections.py` (24) — heading recognition, the card-vs-heading boundary, comment/blank/tail-annotation inheritance, the `About` block, 7 parametrized real-export shapes, the txt↔CSV agreement, the deliberate moxfield-variant gap, and frozen-ness. Gate: 1342 passed, 1 skipped.


- [x] D19.3 Deckbox CSV + print-view HTML adapters. New `decklist/importers.py` with a single public `to_decklist(raw) -> str`: sniff the format, coerce to plaintext, hand off to `parse_sections` (D19.2). Section handling therefore still has exactly one implementation and adding a source never touches it. Text that is already a decklist passes through **byte-for-byte** — round-tripping it through the parser and back would drop the `//` grouping comments people use to organise a list, and buy nothing.

  Format is sniffed from CONTENT, never a filename: uploads arrive with whatever extension the browser guessed, and Deckbox's own export URL ends in `?format=csv` regardless of what comes back (Bug.32). CSV detection requires a count-ish AND a name-ish column in the header, not a bare comma — sniffing on the comma would misroute every decklist containing a legendary creature, since `1 Radagast, the Brown` is a comma-bearing line that is emphatically not a CSV row. Pinned.

  The HTML path came out smaller than the Pending row described. That row said "split on `<br>`, strip tags by regex"; the shipped version only strips tags, because replacing every tag with a newline turns the `<br>` separators AND the `<p><strong>Sideboard:</strong></p>` divider into ordinary lines — and D19.2's colon-tolerant heading rule already reads the latter. So there is no divider special-case, no `<br>` split, and no HTML dependency; the only real work is dropping `<head>`/`<script>`/`<style>` first, since `<title>` sits before the card run and would otherwise parse as a card (pinned). `html.unescape` handles entities (rung 2 — stdlib).

  CSV specifics: `csv.DictReader`, header variants `Count`/`Quantity`/`Qty` and `Name`/`Card`/`Card Name`, `Section` values mapped through a dict with **unrecognised values falling back to mainboard** (a row in the wrong section is recoverable, a dropped row is not). An unparseable `Count` cell degrades to qty 1 rather than failing the import, matching D18.2's posture; zero and negative rows drop. `inventory/deckbox.py:parse_deckbox_csv` was NOT reused — it returns `ParsedDeckboxItem` with foil/edition/price and has no concept of `Section`. The `Commander` block is closed with an explicit `Deck` heading, without which `parse_sections` keeps its current bucket and swallows the entire mainboard into `commander` (pinned).

  Measured result: the CSV and the print-view HTML each resolve to main 81/81 + sideboard 8/19, which is **identical to the txt sibling** from D19.2. All three Deckbox representations of one deck now agree at 100 cards with identical mainboard name sets — pinned as an equality assertion, which is safe here (unlike the Moxfield variants) precisely because these three genuinely ARE the same export in three shapes. Follow-up filed as D19.8 (the `//` normalisation now exists in two places). Pins: new `tests/test_decklist_importers.py` (19) — sniffing incl. the comma-in-a-name trap, plaintext passthrough, CSV sections/variants/degradation/split-names, the commander-swallow guard, HTML head-drop/divider/entities/script, both real exports, and the three-way agreement. Gate: 1361 passed, 1 skipped.


- [x] D19.4 `POST` / `GET` / `PATCH` / `DELETE /decks` — stored-deck CRUD, every write appends a version. New `decks/service.py` (DB layer) + `routers/deck_library.py` (HTTP), mounted after `decks_router` in `main.py`. Deliberately NOT added to `routers/decks.py`, which is already 79 KB and slated for a split (D16.37); paths cannot collide because the stateless surface is all literal sub-paths (`/decks/analyze`, …) while this router owns `/decks`, `/decks/upload` and `/decks/{deck_id}` — pinned by a test that `POST /decks/analyze` still behaves.

  Six routes / six MCP tools: `create_deck` (JSON paste), `upload_deck` (multipart), `list_decks`, `get_deck`, `update_deck`, `delete_deck`. `POST /decks` and `/decks/upload` both run the body through `to_decklist` first, so a pasted CSV or an uploaded print-view HTML is coerced identically to a plaintext list; uploads additionally retain the original bytes in `raw_content` for D19.5's download. Deck name is derived — explicit > the export's own name (Arena's `Name` line, via D19.2) > filename stem > `Untitled deck` — so importing `big-boys-arena.txt` names the deck `Big Boys` without being told.

  **Version dedup is per-deck and load-bearing**: `append_version` compares the incoming text to the current latest and, when identical, returns the existing row with `version_created: false` and issues NO INSERT (pinned by asserting a single query ran). Without it, re-saving an unchanged deck would fill the timeline with no-op entries and make the history useless for spotting real changes. `unresolved_count` is populated by a real `resolve_names` call at write time, so the history records how well each import actually matched.

  Write endpoints all use `engine.begin()`; `engine.begin.assert_called_once()` + `engine.connect.assert_not_called()` is pinned on create / upload / update / delete, and the inverse on the two read routes — D18.3's first draft used `connect()`, whose implicit transaction ROLLS BACK on close, and silently wrote nothing while reporting success. Bug.27 CAST discipline is pinned by walking every emitted statement and asserting no `::` appears. A malformed deck id is rejected BEFORE any query (`CAST('nope' AS uuid)` is a DB-level error, not a 404), pinned by asserting the engine was never touched. The dynamic UPDATE interpolates column names only from the fixed `MUTABLE_FIELDS` tuple, never from caller input; values stay bound.

  Service-URL validation is hostname-suffix per field with a scheme prepended when missing (so `deckbox.org/sets/123` is accepted and `www.deckbox.org` matches), 422 on mismatch — a Moxfield link in `archidekt_url` is what D14.18 would later fetch, so it is a real error rather than a cosmetic one. `DECKLIST_MAX_LEN` (8000) is enforced at write time so an oversized deck fails once on save instead of on every later analyze. `list_decks` reads each deck's current `decklist` via a LATERAL to derive the effective commander but OMITS it from the response — pinned — so browsing a library does not ship every deck body.

  Verified LIVE against the running stack (api rebuilt; no src bind mount, so the image had to be rebuilt to serve the new routes): real `archidekt/colorless_arsenal.txt` uploaded through `POST /decks/upload` → 100 cards, 100 distinct, **0 unresolved** against the populated 34,593-card table, commander correctly derived as `Hope of Ghirapur` from the export's own `Commander` section. PATCH with byte-identical text → `version 1, version_created false`; PATCH with a real change → `version 2`; PATCH restoring the full list → `version 3, total_cards 100`. URL mismatch → 422 with the expected message, malformed and unknown ids → 404, listing shows the deck with its commander and no `decklist` field. Round-tripping the stored `decklist` into `POST /decks/analyze` works but reports `unresolved: ['Mainboard']` — the stateless endpoints still use `parse_decklist`, filed as D19.9. Pins: new `tests/test_decks_library_service.py` (21) and `tests/test_decks_library_router.py` (25), `test_mcp_mount.py` +6 tool names, README +6 endpoint blocks. Gate: 1407 passed, 1 skipped.


- [x] D19.5 Deck version history — list / fetch / download / restore / prune. Five routes on `routers/deck_library.py` + five service functions, five MCP tools (`list_deck_versions`, `get_deck_version`, `download_deck_version`, `restore_deck_version`, `delete_deck_version`).

  **No diff endpoint was built, on purpose.** `POST /decks/diff` (D14.15) already takes two decklist strings, is pure Python and touches no SQL, so version-to-version diff is the caller fetching two versions and POSTing them — verified live below. Building a second diff surface would have duplicated the fold logic for zero new capability.

  `list_versions` selects neither `decklist` nor `raw_content` (pinned by asserting both words are absent from the emitted SQL) — browsing a deck's past must not drag every stored file out of the DB, same call as `uploads.list_uploads`. The listing route 404s on an empty result rather than issuing an existence query first: a real deck always has at least version 1, so empty means no deck, and that invariant saves a round-trip.

  `restore_version` APPENDS the old text as a new version with `source='restore'` — it never truncates, so the restore is itself a visible entry and the version restored from stays put. Restoring the version that is already current needs no special case: it falls through D19.4's ordinary dedup and returns `version_created: false`. `delete_version` refuses the highest-numbered row with 422 because that row IS the deck's current content — deleting it would silently rewrite the deck instead of pruning its past. Deleting an older version leaves a numbering gap (1, 3, 4), which is harmless since `append_version` always uses `latest + 1`.

  Download returns the ORIGINAL bytes under their original filename when the version came from an upload, and synthesises `<name>-v<N>.txt` from the canonical text otherwise (paste / edit / restore have no source file). Media type is `application/octet-stream` for stored bytes rather than a guess from the extension — the attachment disposition forces a save either way and an extension map can only be wrong. `_content_disposition` strips CR / LF / quotes from the filename: it is user-supplied upload input, and a raw CRLF there is a response-header injection — pinned by a test asserting an `X-Injected` header does NOT appear.

  Verified LIVE end-to-end on the real Archidekt deck: history listed v3/v2/v1 with correct sources and counts and no bodies; `GET versions/1/download` returned bytes **`cmp`-identical** to `data/example_deck_exports/archidekt/colorless_arsenal.txt` under `filename="colorless_arsenal.txt"`; the edit version fell back to `Colorless Arsenal-v2.txt` as `text/plain`; restoring v2 appended v4 (`restored_from: 2`, `version_created: true`); restoring the current version returned `created: false`; deleting v4 was refused with the 422; deleting v2 succeeded. Cross-version diff through the existing `/decks/diff` reported 99 added / 1 removed between the 2-card and 100-card versions. Pins: new `tests/test_decks_versions.py` (25), `test_mcp_mount.py` +5 tool names, README +5 endpoint blocks plus a worked diff recipe. Gate: 1432 passed, 1 skipped.


- [x] D19.6 Web Decks tab (eighth tab, between Wishlist and Settings). `web/index.html` gains `#tabDecks` + `#decksPane`; `web/app.js.template` gains the element bindings, the `_setTab` wiring (`decks` added to `TABS`, pane toggle, aria-pressed) and a self-contained `// -------- Decks tab (D19.6) --------` section; `web/style.css` gains ~60 lines. Layout is an import card over a two-column split (deck list | detail), collapsing to one column under 800px since the detail pane needs the width more than the list does. Markup reuses `.inv-sync`, `.inv-paste`, `.inv-search` and `.upload-list`/`.upload-row` wholesale — only genuinely deck-specific classes are new.

  Detail pane carries: metadata line, service links, the version timeline (Download / Restore / Delete per row, with the newest badged `current` and its Restore+Delete buttons omitted rather than disabled, matching the D19.5 rules), a two-select version comparator, and an editable decklist textarea with `Save as new version` that surfaces `version_created` honestly ("No change — identical to the current version" when the dedup fires). The comparator fetches two versions and POSTs them to the EXISTING `/decks/diff`; there is no bespoke diff endpoint and the JS comment says so.

  **Deviation from this row as written, deliberate:** the row promised "per-service pull buttons". None shipped — not even for Moxfield and Archidekt. The pull they would call is D14.18, which is not implemented, so every such button would 404 on click; rendering controls that cannot succeed is exactly the mistake D18.19 is still cleaning up, and the locked decision to omit the Deckbox button for that reason applies with equal force to the other two while the endpoint is missing. All three URLs render as plain links. The pull buttons are now tracked on the D14.18 row, which owns the fetch they depend on. `DECK_LINK_FIELDS` carries the comment explaining the Deckbox case so a future reader does not "fix" it.

  Gate: `make check-web` OK (envsubst render + `node --check`, Bug.29) — no redeclared-const collision, which is the failure mode that has broken this gate before. Verified on the SERVED assets after `make web-reload`, as D18.18 did: all 11 new ids present in the delivered `index.html`, every new JS symbol and route literal present in the delivered `app.js`, the deck CSS present in the delivered `style.css`, and a grep confirming ZERO pull-control markup reaches the browser. Then every endpoint the tab calls was exercised through the nginx `/api` proxy on :8080 rather than the API directly, since the proxy is the real integration risk: list / get / versions / one version / download all 200; upload of `moxfield/big-boys-arena.txt` created "Big Boys" at 88 cards with commander `Radagast of Rhosgobel` (the name and commander both recovered from the export's `About` and `Commander` blocks, matching D19.2's pin of 1 commander + 87 deck cards); PATCH appended v2; the two-fetch + `/decks/diff` comparator returned 1 added / 52 removed / 3 unchanged; restore appended v3 from v1; delete returned `deck_deleted`. **NOT verified in a real browser** — no browser tooling this session, so rendering and interaction are unconfirmed; every asset and network call underneath them is.


- [x] D19.8 De-duplicate the `Fire//Ice` -> `Fire // Ice` normalisation — one definition, three consumers. `normalize_card_name` is now public in `decklist/parser.py` and `parse_decklist` applies it to every entry (after the Bug.3 tail strip, so `Fire//Ice (MH2) 290` loses the annotation first and only then gets its spacing fixed). Both private copies are deleted: `inventory/deckbox.py:_clean_card_name` and `decklist/importers.py:_clean_name`. A grep for the rule (`" // " not in name`) now returns exactly one hit in `src/`.

  **The row's plan was half wrong and the deviation matters.** It proposed moving the rule into `parse_decklist` and deleting both call sites outright. That works for `decklist/importers.py` — its emitted lines flow through `parse_sections` -> `parse_decklist`, so it inherits normalisation for free and its call site really is gone. It does NOT work for `inventory/deckbox.py`: `parse_deckbox_csv` builds `ParsedDeckboxItem`s and resolves them against `cards` directly and never touches `parse_decklist`, so deleting its call site would have silently stopped normalising split-card names on every Deckbox COLLECTION import — a regression, not a cleanup. Shipped instead: it imports the shared callable. Duplication is still eliminated (two definitions -> one), which was the actual point. `test_normalize_card_name_is_the_single_shared_definition` pins `inv_deckbox.normalize_card_name is normalize_card_name` by IDENTITY, so a future re-fork is a test failure rather than a slow drift.

  Net win beyond de-duplication: plaintext decklists get the fix for the first time. Before, only the Deckbox CSV path normalised, so a hand-pasted `1 Fire//Ice` went unresolved. Verified live against the populated 34,593-card table — `POST /decks` with `1 Fire//Ice` stored and then analysed gives `unresolved: []` and resolves to `Fire // Ice`. (The STORED text stays the verbatim `1 Fire//Ice`, which is correct: D19.4 keeps deck text byte-for-byte and normalisation belongs at parse/resolve time, not at rest.)

  The `" // " not in name` guard is what keeps it idempotent, and idempotence is load-bearing here — `/decks/*` responses re-serialize parsed lists and hand them back, so a name that changed on every pass would corrupt decklists the way Bug.33 did. Bug.33's `st.text()` round-trip property was re-run under the seeds it was originally fixed against — 0 / 1 / 7 / 42 / 99 / 1234 — 69 passed each, plus a random-seed run of the two `st.text()` properties. Pins: `tests/test_decklist_parser.py` +9 (7 parametrized spacing cases incl. the partially-spaced hands-off case and the tail-annotation ordering, idempotence, and the shared-identity assertion). Gate: 1441 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.10 Section semantics — `companion` bucket, and commander/companion also in `main`. Author decision resolving the M19 §6.7 drift. `DeckSections` gains `companion: list[DeckEntry]`; `_SECTIONS` maps `companion` to its own bucket instead of folding it into `sideboard` (a deck has at most one, it is tracked as its own field, and folding made it indistinguishable from an ordinary sideboard card). Commander and companion lines are appended to `main` AS THEY ARE READ — while walking, not prepended or tacked on afterwards — so `main` holds the full card pool in source order (pinned by `test_main_preserves_source_order`).

  **`DeckSections` is now deliberately NOT a partition**, and that is the whole risk of this deliverable: `commander + main + sideboard` double-counts, `main + sideboard` is the deck. Documented loudly in both the module and the class docstring (with the two-line correct/wrong contrast), and pinned by `test_sections_are_not_a_partition`, which asserts the naive sum is 46 where the real total is 44 — so the footgun is a failing test rather than a comment nobody reads.

  Consumer audit found exactly ONE double-count site, `decks/service.py:decklist_counts`, which spanned `[*commander, *main, *sideboard]` and now spans `[*main, *sideboard]`. Every other consumer was checked and is unaffected: `effective_commander` reads `.commander` only; `routers/deck_library.py:_derive_name` reads `.name` only; `decklist/importers.py:_assemble` builds from CSV row buckets (a plain `dict[str, list[str]]`) and never from a `DeckSections`, verified rather than assumed; `routers/decks.py`'s many `.commander` hits are all `body.commander` on the stateless request models; `format_decklist` at `routers/decks.py:715` takes `parse_decklist` entries. Notably NOTHING re-serialises a `DeckSections` back to text anywhere, so the "writes the commander twice" hazard is latent rather than live — which is exactly why the docstrings had to be emphatic. The web UI reads server-computed `total_cards`, so the one service fix covers it. Regression pinned in `test_decks_library_service.py::test_counts_do_not_double_count_commander_or_companion`.

  Fixture pins RE-MEASURED off the files with a standalone script that does not import `parse_sections`, not adjusted by arithmetic. Confirmed the predicted direction exactly: archidekt main 99/99 -> **100/100**, arena main 50/87 -> **51/88**, and Deckbox + all four Moxfield files **unchanged** — the unchanged files being the control that shows the change is scoped to exports that actually carry a `Commander` section. `_FIXTURES` gains a companion column (all zero — no reference export has a Companion section, which is why the drift existed). The Deckbox three-way agreement still holds. `test_moxfield_variants_of_one_deck_are_not_assumed_equal` had to drop its `commander + main` sum, which now double-counts by construction.

  Live: rebuilt the api image and imported `archidekt/colorless_arsenal.txt` end-to-end against the populated 34,593-card table — `total_cards: 100` (not 101), `distinct_cards: 100`, `unresolved_count: 0`, commander derived as `Hope of Ghirapur`. Gate: 1445 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.11 `decks.companion` column + CRUD + web. Completes the D19.10 author decision: a companion is now a first-class field, not a sideboard card. The override is not decoration: no reference export in `data/example_deck_exports/` carries a `Companion` section at all, so derivation alone would leave the field permanently null for every real file today.

New surface is `companion` on `POST /decks` (JSON), `POST /decks/upload` (`Form`), and `PATCH /decks/{id}`, plus `companion` / `companion_override` on every deck payload and `companion` on the list rows. Web tab shows it after the commander in both the library row and the detail header.

Rewritten to `re.search(r"(?m)^\s*decklist\s+\w", ddl) is None` — a column declaration, not a bare word — and verified it still trips on a real `decklist text NOT NULL,` line. The old form was fragile in a way that would have punished the next person to comment the DDL.

Live end-to-end after an api rebuild: a deck with a `Companion` section derived `Lurrus of the Dream-Den` with `companion_override: null`, counted **45 cards / 5 distinct** (1 commander + 1 companion + 38 + 1 + 4 — no double count, matching the D19.10 unit pin exactly), and a subsequent `PATCH` override returned `Yorion, Sky Nomad` in both fields. The multipart path was exercised separately with the real `Iroh gift` Deckbox CSV plus a `companion` form field: 100 cards / 89 distinct, matching D19.3's pinned CSV shape, with `commander: null` — the documented case of an export carrying no Commander section. - [x] D19.9 `/decks/*` reads sections — phantom `unresolved` headings gone. All fourteen `parse_decklist` call sites in `routers/decks.py` now go through one of three helpers: `_maindeck_entries` (sections `.main`), `_whole_list_entries` (`.main + .sideboard`), or `_legality_body_entries` (`.main` minus the commander). The choice is per-endpoint policy, NOT a blanket rule — mainboard only for the eleven analysis/simulation endpoints, main+sideboard for `price` / `export` / `diff`, and body-plus-separate-sideboard for `legality`; drift filed with the reasoning for each. Live before: `POST /decks/analyze` on `archidekt/colorless_arsenal.txt` returned `unresolved: ['Mainboard']`. Live after: `unresolved: []`, and all three stored decks report `total_cards=100` with `unresolved: []`.

  The sharp edge was `legality`. `check_legality` documents `qty_by_lower` as the deck BODY with the commander excluded and asserts `expected_body = 99 if commander_ci is not None else 100`; since D19.10 the commander sits INSIDE `main`, so handing `main` over unmodified would have reported a bogus `deck_size` violation on a perfectly legal deck — a regression introduced by the fix, not present before it. `_legality_body_entries` subtracts by QUANTITY rather than by name, so a commander that also appears as a legal singleton elsewhere keeps its other copy. Verified live: the Archidekt export now validates at `deck_size=99`, `expected=100`, zero size violations, `legal: true` — with no `commander` argument passed at all, because a `Commander` heading now supplies it. An explicitly-passed `commander` wins over the parsed one (pinned).

  **Zero pre-existing tests needed changing** — all 1452 prior tests passed untouched. That is the compatibility property doing its job: a heading-less decklist puts everything in `main`, so every existing caller pasting a plain list behaves exactly as before. Pinned directly across seven endpoints rather than left implicit.

  Scope notes: `/decks/bracket` does NOT take a commander separately (`BracketRequest` carries only `decklist`), so there was nothing to wire there — the brief's assumption was wrong. A pre-existing Bug.27 violation sits at `_COMBOS_COUNT_SQL` (`:deck_oids::uuid[]` instead of `CAST`); untouched, out of scope, worth its own row. Second drift filed on `commander_color_identity` being `null` for a colorless commander — pre-existing, but D19.9's inference makes it reachable without the caller knowing, so it is now pinned rather than accidental. Pins: new `tests/test_decks_sections_routing.py` (20) — the two live repros against real Archidekt and Arena exports, the heading-less compatibility property across seven endpoints, sideboard exclusion across four, price/export inclusion, the legality commander-exclusion and explicit-wins precedence, inline-100 still accepted, and the colorless-commander wart. Gate: 1472 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.16 `decks.folder` — nested deck folders as a normalised path string. Column declared inline in `CREATE TABLE decks` (matching how `companion` shipped in D19.11) with the in-place `ALTER` recipe in the commit body per CLAUDE.md, plus `decks_folder_idx ON decks (folder text_pattern_ops)`. A folder is NOT a table: it has no independent existence, it *is* the set of decks sharing a path prefix, which removes the only two failure modes a `folders` table would have guarded against — a folder cannot be orphaned and a cycle cannot be constructed. Priced cost, documented in README and the schema comment: case variants and typos silently create phantom folders (`Commander` and `commander` are two), stored as-typed rather than case-folded because folding surprises anyone who capitalises deliberately, with D19.17's subtree rename as the mitigation.

  `text_pattern_ops` is load-bearing and was PROVEN so rather than assumed: `EXPLAIN` against the live PG 17 stack shows Postgres rewriting `folder LIKE 'Commander/%'` into an index range scan (`folder ~>=~ 'Commander/' AND folder ~<~ 'Commander0'`) on `decks_folder_idx`. A default btree opclass does not serve a prefix LIKE — the index would be built and never used. Same reasoning as `cards_name_lower_pattern_idx`. Whole migration verified inside `BEGIN; … ROLLBACK;` via `docker compose exec db psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1`.

  `normalize_folder` trims the string and every segment, collapses repeated and edge separators, maps anything normalising to nothing onto `NULL`, and RAISES on control characters (a newline breaks any tree the UI renders) or a path over 256 chars — validation at the boundary so a bad path is a 422, not a row nothing can display. `folder_like_prefix` escapes `%`, `_` and the escape character itself and anchors on a trailing slash, so `Commander` cannot match `CommanderX` and a folder legitimately named `50%_budget` cannot turn one subtree query into a full-table match; callers pair it with `ESCAPE '\\'`. Both traps pinned with adversarial names, and D19.17 inherits the helper.

  `folder` joins `MUTABLE_FIELDS`, so `PATCH` reaches it through the existing shared tuple with no new plumbing. `GET /decks?folder=` filters exactly, `?include_subfolders=true` widens to the subtree, and `?folder=` **present but empty** means unfiled — deliberately a different request from omitting the parameter, pinned because the distinction is exactly what a later refactor would flatten. New `GET /decks/folders` returns every path with `deck_count` / `total_deck_count` / `name` / `depth` plus `unfiled_count`, with ancestors DERIVED: a deck at `Commander/Budget` yields a `Commander` node even with no deck directly in it, without which a caller gets a leaf whose parent is missing and cannot build a tree.

  **Route-ordering trap, pinned:** `/decks/folders` must be declared BEFORE `/decks/{deck_id}` or FastAPI matches it as a deck id and 422s on the UUID parse. The pin asserts declaration order on `deck_library.router.routes` rather than `app.routes` — the latter holds `_IncludedRouter` wrappers whose leaf paths are not exposed, which cost a debugging round to discover. A second behavioural pin asserts `get_deck` is never awaited for that path. One pre-existing test file changed: `DECK_ROW` in `tests/test_decks_library_service.py` gained `"folder": None` — a fake `decks` row gaining a column the deliverable adds, i.e. representing reality, not accommodating a bug. Pins: new `tests/test_decks_folders.py` (35), `tests/test_decks_schema.py` +2, `test_mcp_mount.py` +`list_deck_folders`. Gate: 1509 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.17 `POST /decks/folders/rename` — one-call subtree move, and the repair path for the phantom folders D19.16 priced in. `UPDATE decks SET folder = :new || substr(folder, :old_len + 1) WHERE folder = :old OR folder LIKE :old_prefix ESCAPE '\\'`. **The WHERE shape IS the deliverable:** the obvious `LIKE :old || '%'` also matches `CommanderX`, silently re-filing an unrelated sibling that merely shares a name prefix. Descendants are rewritten by OFFSET rather than string replacement — a blind replace turns `Commander/x/Commander` into `Legacy/x/Legacy` instead of `Legacy/x/Commander`. Both pinned, and the sibling trap was then **proven against the live stack with real data**: three decks filed at `Commander/Budget`, `Commander/Cedh` and `CommanderX`, rename `Commander`→`Legacy` reported `decks_moved: 2` and left `CommanderX` untouched. `folder_like_prefix` is reused from D19.16 rather than re-derived, so the escaping of `%` / `_` (a folder legitimately named `50%_budget`) has one implementation.

  `to` omitted/empty UNFILES the subtree, which is also how a folder is deleted — there is no folder row to remove. Renaming an unknown folder is not an error, it reports `decks_moved: 0` (verified live). Missing `from` raises rather than running: without that guard the UPDATE would match every unfiled deck. `updated_at` is deliberately NOT bumped — `update_deck` moves it so the library's newest-touched-first ordering tracks real edits, and a bulk reorganisation is not per-deck activity; bumping every row would reshuffle the whole listing for a purely organisational change. Pinned so it is visibly a decision rather than an omission. Write route, `engine.begin()` pinned per D18.3.

  **Gap found only by going live:** `list_decks` builds its own summary row rather than calling `_deck_payload`, so `GET /decks` returned no `folder` even though `GET /decks/{id}` did. A client could filter by folder but not show which folder a deck was in — most of what D19.18's tree needs. Every mock-based test passed throughout; only a real request surfaced it. Fixed and pinned with `test_list_rows_carry_the_folder`, which is now the guard against the two payload builders drifting again. Pins: new `tests/test_decks_folder_rename.py` (16) — WHERE shape, the bare-prefix counter-example, metacharacter escaping, offset-not-replace, unfiling, rowcount, unknown folder, normalisation of both sides, `updated_at` untouched, both raise-guards, plus router count/transaction/422/unfile/route-order. `tests/test_decks_folders.py` +1, `test_mcp_mount.py` +`rename_deck_folder`. Gate: 1525 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.19 Derived `deck_cards` index — the D19.1 upgrade path, triggered exactly as predicted. `deck_cards(deck_id, section, card_name, oracle_id NULL, qty)` + indexes on `deck_id` and `oracle_id`. The decklist text stays the source of truth; these rows are regenerated from it inside the SAME transaction as `append_version`, so rolling back a version rolls back the index with it and the two cannot disagree.

  **Partition invariant — the way this could have gone silently wrong.** `parse_sections` is deliberately NOT a partition (D19.10: commander and companion sit inside `main`), so writing its buckets straight into rows double-counts them and `SUM(qty)` reports a 101-card Commander deck — quietly corrupting every coverage percentage derived from the index. `deck_cards` IS a partition: commander rows, main-minus-commander-and-companion rows, sideboard rows. Verified live on three real decks — index total equals the stored version total at 100 each, zero unresolved rows.

  The quantity-subtraction `_legality_body_entries` did inline is now `decklist/sections.py:subtract_entries`, used by both callers. Semantics stay per-caller ON PURPOSE: legality removes only the commander (a companion occupies a sideboard slot, and folding it in would silently change a shipped deck-size check), `deck_cards` removes both. Subtraction is by quantity, not by name — pinned with a 1-commander + 2-in-body case where name-based removal under-counts by two. `oracle_id` nullable with `card_name` retained so an unresolved name still counts in the coverage DENOMINATOR. Rebuild is delete-then-insert, never upsert (D18.2's duplicate-stacking trap); resolution is inline `LEFT JOIN LATERAL … LIMIT 1`, LATERAL rather than a plain join because `cards` can hold several rows per name (reversible faces, §3.4) and a plain join would multiply the deck.

  `rebuild_all_deck_cards` ships and is pinned but is NOT called by anything — filed as D19.24, because the natural trigger is a new refresh stage and that touches `ALL_STAGES`, the DAG, `/health` telemetry, the web stage checkboxes and their pins. Backfill was run manually against the live stack (3 decks); the recipe is in the commit body. One of my own test expectations was wrong and was corrected by reasoning rather than by bending the assert. Pins: new `tests/test_decks_cards_index.py` (12), `tests/test_decks_schema.py` +1. Gate: 1539 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.20 `GET /decks/{id}/cards` — the deck as a table, not a blob. Flat rows with a `section` field (`commander` / `main` / `sideboard`) rather than a grouped payload, because sorting the WHOLE deck by ownership is the point and grouping fights it. Each row carries `qty`, the card `id` for linking to the existing card detail pane, `oracle_id`, `type_line`, `mana_cost`, `cmc`, `price_usd`, and quantity-aware `qty_owned` / `qty_missing` / `on_wishlist` / `resolved`. Reads the DERIVED index (D19.19), so nothing re-parses the decklist per request — one existence check plus one query.

  A dedicated endpoint rather than reusing `POST /decks/analyze` for two reasons that are both correctness, not convenience: analyze reads MAINBOARD ONLY (D19.9), so a deck's own card table would silently omit its sideboard; and the UI should not have to POST a decklist back to read the deck it just fetched. Ownership uses `SUM(quantity)` over `inventory`, never a row read — `inventory` is one row per PRINTING (D18.1) and a row read keeps only one edition's quantity, which is exactly the silent bug D18.5 hit. `qty_owned` is clamped to `qty` so owning extras cannot make `qty_missing` negative. Unresolved names still appear (`id: null`, `resolved: false`) so the table always agrees with the deck's card count instead of inflating coverage; an empty deck reports 100% rather than dividing by zero.

  Verified live against the 34,593-card table: `colorless_arsenal` reports 100 cards (1 commander + 99 main) at **56.0% coverage — identical to the `coverage_pct` `/decks/analyze` reported for the same deck before any of this existed**, an independent cross-check of the whole chain. Both `Iroh gift` decks report 81 main + 19 sideboard at 97%; note that number legitimately differs from analyze's, which excludes the sideboard. One of my own test fixtures was wrong (`_row(card_name=...)` left `name` at its default, collapsing two rows onto one key) and was fixed rather than the assertion loosened. Pins: new `tests/test_decks_cards_endpoint.py` (14), `test_mcp_mount.py` +`list_deck_cards`. Gate: 1553 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.21 `GET /decks?sort=coverage` — library sorted by how much of each deck you already own, plus `sort=name` and `direction=asc|desc`. **This is the query D19.1 said could not be done against text storage**, and it is now one statement: inventory folded ONCE into a CTE, joined to `deck_cards`, grouped per deck. A per-row LATERAL would have done decks×cards index lookups per page. Sorting is on the RATIO, not the raw owned count — a fully-built 40-card deck must outrank a 60-of-100 one — with `LEAST` clamping per card so owning five copies of a singleton contributes one and coverage cannot exceed 100%. `NULLS LAST` under either direction: a deck with no indexed rows (predating D19.19) must never outrank a real one by being NULL.

  Sort keys are a WHITELIST mapped to expressions, never interpolated caller input — `sort` reaches SQL as text, so an unknown key raises and the router 422s (pinned with a `'; DROP TABLE decks; --` argument). ORDER BY is pinned to appear BEFORE LIMIT: sorting after pagination would only reorder one page, and the top-coverage deck might never appear at all. Rows carry `owned_cards` and `coverage_pct` regardless of sort, because sorting by an invisible number is not usable; `coverage_pct` is `None` rather than `0` for an unindexed deck, since "not indexed yet" is not "you own none of it". Default sort is unchanged and pinned, so existing callers are unaffected.

  Verified live: descending gives `Iroh gift 97% | Iroh gift 97% | colorless_arsenal 56%`, ascending reverses it, `sort=bogus` returns 422. The percentages are **identical to D19.20's endpoint and to `/decks/analyze`** — a third independent path landing on the same numbers. Two pre-existing test fixtures gained `cov_total` / `cov_owned`: fake list rows gaining the two columns this deliverable adds to the SELECT, i.e. representing reality, not accommodating a bug; no assertion was altered. One of my own test assertions checked the SELECT aliases instead of the CTE's own columns and was corrected. Pins: new `tests/test_decks_sort_coverage.py` (14). Gate: 1567 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.12 Served-vs-shipped gate — `make check-served`. `check-web` renders the template to a TEMP FILE and runs `node --check`; it proves the template PARSES and structurally cannot prove the container serves it, because it never makes an HTTP request. The gap is real: `web/Dockerfile` COPYs `index.html`/`style.css`/`helpers.js`/`app.js.template` into the IMAGE at build time and `10-render-app-js.sh` renders `app.js` at container START, so nothing is bind-mounted and `docker compose restart web` re-serves the same stale bytes. Four web deliverables shipped green while the browser got pre-edit assets.

  New `scripts/check_served.sh` fetches all four assets from the running nginx and diffs them against the working tree, naming each stale file with byte counts and the first differing lines. `app.js` is rendered locally through envsubst using the DEFAULT_Q read OUT OF the running container (`docker compose exec web printenv`) rather than guessed — rendering with a different value would report a mismatch that is not real and train everyone to ignore the check. Two targets: `check-served` hard-fails when no stack is up (an explicit request implies a stack), `check-served-soft` skips LOUDLY and is what `make check` runs, so the gate stays usable with no stack without ever silently passing.

  **Proven to fail, which is the deliverable — a gate that has never gone red is not a gate.** Full cycle captured in the commit body: baseline GREEN (4/4 match) → append one comment line to `web/style.css` → RED naming `style.css`, served 38994 vs tree 39023 bytes, showing the added line → `make web-reload` → GREEN at 39023 → revert the tree edit without reloading → RED again showing the removed line, so detection is symmetric rather than one-directional. The first RED run also exposed a genuine bug in the script itself: `diff` exits 1 on difference and `set -e` + `pipefail` killed it before printing the diagnostic — the one moment it exists for. Fixed with an explicit `|| true` and a comment saying why.

  CLAUDE.md framed `web-reload` as a `.env`-only step; it now has its own section stating ANY `web/*` edit needs it, with the baked-not-mounted mechanism spelled out and the Runbook entries corrected. Pins: new `tests/test_check_served_gate.py` (8) — script exists and is executable, `make check` actually depends on it, a standalone hard-fail target exists, bash syntax valid, unreachable stack FAILS by default, unreachable stack skips LOUDLY in soft mode (a silent skip is indistinguishable from a pass), all four shipped assets covered, and DEFAULT_Q read from the container rather than guessed. Gate: 1575 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.14 Bug.27 `:x::type` — **not consistency debt, a live 500.** The row (and the brief) recorded these four sites as harmless on the grounds that SQLAlchemy leaves `::` alone and the queries were live-verified. Both halves were wrong, and checking before refactoring is what caught it. `text("... :deck_oids::uuid[]")` registers a bind named `deck_oid` — the trailing `s` is SWALLOWED — then `construct_params({'deck_oids': [...]})` returns `{}`, the compiled statement keeps a literal `:deck_oids`, and Postgres answers `syntax error at or near ":"`. Verified against the running stack: `/decks/combos`, `/decks/bracket` and `/decks/upgrade` ALL returned **500** before the fix and 200 after (16 combos, bracket 5, 10 upgrade swaps on a real deck). The existing pin in `test_decks_synergy.py` only inspected SQL containing `tag_pairs`, which is exactly why four sites and three endpoints went unguarded.

  Fixed all four to `CAST(:x AS uuid[])` (`decks.py` 1150, 1673, 1924, 1933) plus the endpoint `description=` at 1269, which quoted `:deck_oids::uuid[]` as though it were the real SQL — user-facing API documentation teaching the form that breaks. New `tests/test_bug27_repo_scan.py` (5) scans the WHOLE package rather than one query: no `:name::` in any non-comment line, plus mechanism pins proving the CAST form keeps the bind and the `::` form both mangles the name and drops the caller's parameter, so nobody can "simplify" the CAST away later. The scanner deliberately allows the generic `:X::TYPE` placeholder, because prose that teaches the rule (`routers/catalog.py`) has to quote the wrong form to name it. Gate: 1580 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.13 `?tab=` now reaches all eight tabs, and a deep-link loads its data. `_bootFromUrl` mapped the param through a hardcoded ternary chain (`deck` / `compare` / `combos`, else `search`), so `?tab=decks|inventory|wishlist|settings` all landed on Cards — and so did a plain browser REFRESH on any of them. Replaced with a registry derived from the MARKUP (`document.querySelectorAll("[data-tab]")`), so a future tab is deep-linkable by construction rather than by remembering to edit this function; the literal list survives only as a fallback for a DOM with no tabs.

  Second half: `_setTab` toggled panes but did NOT load data — `refreshDecksList()` hung off the nav click handler alone, which is why a `_setTab("decks")` rendered an empty pane and was briefly mistaken for a rendering bug during D19.12's browser pass. Loaders moved into a `_TAB_LOADERS` map dispatched from `_setTab`, so click and deep-link take one path; the five click handlers collapse to bare `_setTab(name)`.

  **Two TDZ bugs, the second found only by reading the browser console.** `_setTab` now touches module-level `let`s, and `_bootFromUrl()` ran partway up a 3.4k-line file. First `_invLoaded`/`_wlLoaded` (hoisted beside `_combosLoaded`), then the headless run surfaced `?tab=inventory` throwing `Cannot access '_invPage' before initialization` — while the DOM looked perfectly correct, which is exactly why the console check earned its place. Hoisting each offending variable is whack-a-mole, so `_bootFromUrl()` now runs as the LAST top-level statement, after every declaration and listener. Pinned as "no top-level code after boot".

  Verified in headless Chrome against the served stack: all 8 tabs activate their own pane from `?tab=`, `?tab=bogus` falls back to Cards, `?tab=decks` renders **3 deck rows** (proving the loader runs on a deep-link, not just the pane toggle), and 0 JS console errors on decks/inventory/settings. Pins: new `tests/test_web_tab_deeplinks.py` (8) — 8 tabs in markup, no hardcoded chain, registry DOM-derived, `_setTab` dispatches loaders, loader map covers every fetching tab, boot runs last, guards declared before boot. Gate: 1588 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.15 `.ext-link` unscoped — Download links were unreadable on dark. Root cause was selector scoping, not a colour value: the rule read `.detail-pane .ext-link, #combosDetail .ext-link`, and there is no generic `a { }` in `style.css` (only `a:focus-visible` inside a focus-ring group), so every anchor outside those two containers inherited the user-agent's default link blue against `--bg: #1a1a1a`. Unscoped to a bare `.ext-link` rather than adding a third scoped selector — the scoping bought nothing and the next component to reuse the class would have hit the same wall.

  **More call sites than the two reported.** The class is used 7 times; the import-history Download, the deck version Download, the inventory summary's Deckbox link and D19.11's deck service-URL links were all unstyled, not just the two spotted in the browser pass. Specificity drop from (0,2,0) to (0,1,0) checked for regressions: the only other rule touching a bare `<a>` is `a:focus-visible`, which cannot conflict. Verified visually in headless Chrome, dark mode, before and after — Download rendered as underlined default-blue text beside its styled `Delete` sibling, and now renders as the same bordered chip. Pins: new `tests/test_web_ext_link_style.py` (3) — the rule is not container-scoped, it sets an explicit themed colour rather than inheriting, and the class has enough users that a future re-scoping has to confront how many places it breaks. One of my own assertions grabbed the first textual `.ext-link` (which landed in the new explanatory comment) instead of the rule, and was re-anchored on a line-start regex. Gate: 1591 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.18 Web folder tree for the Decks tab. Collapsible tree rendered from `GET /decks/folders`, filtering the deck list on selection, with move-deck (PATCH) and rename-folder (D19.17) controls. **Ancestors are NOT re-derived client-side** — the endpoint already supplies them, so a deck at `Commander/Budget` yields a `Commander` node with `deck_count: 0` and `total_deck_count: 2`, and rebuilding that rule in JS would be a second implementation free to disagree with the server's. Both counts render when they differ (`0 / 2`), so a parent holding no decks of its own still reads sensibly. Indentation is driven by a `--depth` custom property set from the server-supplied `depth`, so nesting never depends on the CSS re-parsing a path.

  Selection is three-valued via named constants `_FOLDER_ALL` / `_FOLDER_UNFILED`, matching the three distinct requests the API exposes: omitting `folder=` (no filter), `?folder=` present-but-empty (unfiled only), and `?folder=X&include_subfolders=true` (subtree). Flattening the last two either hides every unfiled deck or ignores the selection, so unfiled gets its own top-level bucket and is never hidden. Collapse hides a node when ANY ancestor is collapsed, not just its direct parent. Renaming to blank unfiles the subtree — how a folder is deleted, since a folder has no row of its own — and the selection follows the rename rather than pointing at a path that no longer exists.

  Pins: new `tests/test_web_folder_tree.py` (14). Thirteen are static reads of the template; the fourteenth **executes** `_deckFolderQuery` under node (already a hard dependency of `check-web`) across all three selection states plus a `50%_budget` name, because the three-valued distinction is exactly what a later refactor flattens and a substring pin would not notice. Two of my own assertions guessed identifiers that did not exist and were re-anchored on the real mechanism. Browser-verified after `make web-reload`: `All decks 3 / CommanderX 1 / Legacy 0-2 / Budget 1 / Cedh 1 / Unfiled 0`, correct indentation, working collapse toggle. `make check` caught the stale serve first — D19.12 earning its keep on its first real use. Gate: 1605 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.22 Web: deck card table replacing the decklist text. Sectioned table (Commander / Mainboard / Sideboard) from `GET /decks/{id}/cards`, rows clickable through to the existing card detail pane via `selectCard()` — reusing that pipeline rather than duplicating the /cards/:id fetch, similar-cards list and tag rendering. **Read-only per the author decision:** the `deckListText` textarea and its "Save as new version" control are gone from the pane. `PATCH /decks/{id}` with a decklist is UNTOUCHED in the API — shipped, tested, MCP-exposed surface that agents and curl callers use; withdrawing a UI affordance is not deleting a capability, and the folder move still PATCHes.

  Ownership reuses the EXISTING icon vocabulary rather than inventing a second one for the same concept: 📦 for inventory and ⭐ for wishlist, matching `_ownedBadge` on the Inventory and Wishlist tabs, with a `qty_owned/qty` count beside it. The count is not decoration — it carries the same information as the icon for anyone who cannot distinguish full from partial. Partial is dimmed via `filter: saturate(...)`, following `.wanted-icon`'s precedent. Rows with `resolved: false` carry `id: null`; they still render (they count toward the deck total, so hiding them would make the table disagree with its own summary) but emit no `data-id`, so a click can never fetch `/cards/null`. Sorting is client-side on any column including ownership, with SECTION always the primary key — sorting across sections would mix the commander into the 99.

Fixed, and pinned twice: once on the correct name, once with a scan asserting every module-level function called from the table's render path is actually defined — closing the class, not just the instance. Four of my own assertions were anchored on the wrong function and were re-anchored. No pre-existing test changed.

- [x] D19.23 Web: version compare as a git-style diff table. Replaces the three grouped lists with one PR-style table over the existing `POST /decks/diff` (D14.15) — zero backend change. Removals render red, additions green, and a quantity change renders as the PAIR a code diff shows for a modified line (`−3 Sol Ring · was 3` then `+1 Sol Ring · now 1`) rather than a bare delta, which would hide the starting point.

  **Colour is never the only signal.** Each row carries a `+` / `−` sign in its own cell, and the colour merely reinforces it — roughly 1 in 12 men cannot reliably separate red from green, and a diff encoding add/remove solely in hue is unreadable to them. Both themes get their own colour pair (light and dark, plus explicit `html[data-theme=...]` overrides for the manual toggle); a single hardcoded pair would be unreadable in one of the two. Rows use a background rather than only a text colour, so the band is visible independently of the sign.

Two of my own assertions pinned literal class names that are built as `deck-diff-${kind}` and were re-anchored on the template plus both kind arguments. No pre-existing test changed. Gate: 1631 passed, 1 skipped.

- [x] D19.25 Web: sort the deck library by inventory coverage. Sort control on the Decks pane offering `recently touched` (default, unchanged), `name` and `owned %`, with an independent direction selector — descending answers "which deck can I build tonight?", ascending answers "what do I still need to buy?". **Sorting goes through the API** (D19.21's `?sort=&direction=`), never a client-side reorder: the list is paginated, so reordering the rows already on screen would leave the top-coverage deck stranded on page 2. Each row now shows its coverage, because sorting by a number the reader cannot see is not usable.

It renders as "coverage unknown", since 0% claims you own none of it, which is a different and wrong statement. - [x] D19.24 `deck_cards` refresh stage — the derived index now maintains itself. Option (a) of the three weighed in the D19.19 row: a seventh stage rather than a boot-time call or an admin endpoint, because it is the only one that fires exactly when resolution can CHANGE (after `ingest` commits) and it doubles as the backfill for decks predating the index. Joins the post-ingest fan-out beside `classify` / `combos`, not the independent `tags`, since resolution reads `cards`. **Best-effort** like its neighbours — a deck-index failure must never lose a completed card ingest or strand the advisory lock; `ingest` and `embed→hnsw` remain the only fatal stages. Uses `engine.begin()`, never `connect()` (D18.3: `connect()` opens a txn ROLLED BACK on close, so the rebuild would write nothing and report success).

  Everything that had to move with the stage: `ALL_STAGES`, the `stages` filter validation, `/health` per-stage telemetry, the README stage TABLE and the ASCII DAG diagram, the two README prose lists of stage names, the `POST /admin/refresh` `stages` parameter description (which enumerated the valid names and would otherwise have taught six of seven), and the web Settings tab's checkbox grid — six boxes became seven, rendered with its `← ingest` dependency label and confirmed SERVED (7 boxes in the delivered `index.html`).

  Verified live, and more thoroughly than intended: a mis-typed trigger (`stages` is a QUERY parameter, not a JSON body field — the body was silently ignored) kicked off a full pipeline, which then exercised the stage in its real position rather than in isolation. Every stage came back clean: `ingest` 109953, `tags` 555315, `embed` 1, `classify` 34593, `combos` 1999, **`deck_cards` 3**, `hnsw`, no errors, `cards_count` unchanged at 34593. The isolated path was then verified properly with `POST /admin/refresh?stages=deck_cards` → `stages: [('deck_cards', 3, None)]`, only that stage running. Coverage survived both runs identically (97/97/56) and the card table still reports 100 cards at 56% — a rebuild that silently emptied the index would otherwise look like success. Pins: new `tests/test_refresh_deck_cards_stage.py` (8) — registration, post-ingest ordering, telemetry rows, best-effort survival with the ingest result intact, select-alone, skip-when-unselected, the default body rebuilding every deck, and the write-transaction guard. - [x] D19.26 Import form gains the metadata fields. Three URL inputs plus a description box on the Decks tab's import card, wired on BOTH paths — multipart `POST /decks/upload` via `fd.append`, JSON `POST /decks` via the payload — behind a collapsed `<details>` "…more details" reusing the same disclosure pattern as the existing paste box, so the common case stays a file picker and one button. **Zero backend work**: every field was already accepted; the gap was purely that no input existed, so an imported deck could only get a description or a Moxfield link via curl.

  Both paths go through ONE `_deckImportMeta()` collector reading a single `DECK_META_INPUTS` list — two copies of "which fields exist" is exactly what drifts when a field is added later, and the same list drives the post-import reset (without which the next import silently inherits the previous deck's links). Blank values are OMITTED rather than sent as `""`: the columns mean NULL when unset, and an empty string would sail past D19.4's hostname check as a stored non-URL. **No client-side hostname validation** — pinned by asserting the three domains appear nowhere in the collector. One validator, server-side, or the rule drifts between tiers; the existing handler already surfaces the 422's `details`.

Verified through a real browser via CDP: expanded the disclosure, filled all four fields, submitted, got `Imported "ZZ ui meta probe" — 2 cards, 0 unresolved`, fields cleared to `["","","",""]`, zero console errors; `GET /decks/{id}` then returned all four values persisted and `versions: 1` (an import creates exactly its own first version). Wrong-host URL confirmed live: `moxfield_url: https://archidekt.com/...` → `422 moxfield_url must point at moxfield.com`. Pins: new `tests/test_web_deck_import_meta.py` (13). - [x] D19.27 Editable metadata panel on the deck detail. Name, format, folder, commander, companion, description and the three URLs, saved via one `PATCH` through the shared `MUTABLE_FIELDS` tuple. Rendered generically from a `DECK_META_FIELDS` table carrying each column's label and whether blanking it is allowed, so adding a field later touches one list rather than a renderer, a payload builder and a save handler. Sits under the existing `_renderDeckLinks` strip, which is reused rather than duplicated — the URLs read as link chips when set and as inputs inside the panel.

  **Does NOT reverse D19.22's read-only decision.** That removed decklist editing because versions come only from imports and syncs; this touches METADATA only, and `update_deck` appends a version solely when `decklist` is supplied. `_deckMetaPayload` is pinned to never carry a decklist, and the live round trip confirms `versions` stayed at 1 across three saves.

  Blank handling is the subtle part, and is executed under node rather than substring-matched because a static pin cannot tell `null` from `""`: a blank NULLABLE field is sent as `null` (omitting it would keep the old value; `""` would store a non-URL that D19.4's validator would then have to special-case), while a blank `name` is DROPPED entirely since `decks.name` is NOT NULL. Verified live — clearing the Archidekt URL actually unset it and its link chip disappeared.
